# makun-ai — Project Map (Phase 1)

- **Date:** 2026-10-10
- **Repositories documented:** `C:/Users/hp/Desktop/makun-ai-feature-dev-2026` (the `makun-ai` service, branch `feature-dev-2026`) and `C:/Users/hp/Desktop/makun-ai-oc-config-master` (the `makun-ai-oc-config` GitOps repo, branch `master`)
- **Status:** complete, verified against source
- **Produced by:** twelve survey readers (one per code slice, every non-test file read in full), one orchestrator that measured the repos with `find`, `wc`, `grep` and Python `ast`, six section writers and one assembler; library behaviour was checked by reading the locked package versions in a scratch virtualenv (see section 12)
- **Scope:** Phase 1 of a four-phase documentation set (map, deep docs per module, flows per endpoint/job, learning path); the index is `docs/00-README.md`
- **Confidential:** this document describes company source code; it contains no secret values

# 0. How to use this document

1. Read sections 1 to 4 in order first: what the product is, what it is built with, which processes start it, and how a request, a job, a call and a webhook move through it. Sections 5 to 8 are reference tables and one diagram. Section 9 is a reading order for the code and section 10 the short list of files that matter most. Section 11 lists what is broken, dead or inconsistent. Section 12 says how every number and library claim was checked.
2. Company-written documentation already exists in `docs/architecture/`. This map was produced independently, from the code, and does not reuse those documents; where the two disagree, section 11.11 lists each disagreement as "doc claim vs code reality" with file and line numbers.
3. "verified" next to a claim means it was measured or read in the library source on 2026-10-10. "not verified" means the claim rests on reading the project code only and was not checked against library source. "(not re-verified)" in section 11 means the line number comes from a survey and was not re-read by the section author.
4. Secret values are never printed. Secrets are referred to by environment-variable NAME only (for example `GROQ_API_KEY`), and hardcoded defaults in source are described as "redacted". Hostnames and URLs without credentials are shown as they appear in code.
5. File paths are relative to the main repo root `C:/Users/hp/Desktop/makun-ai-feature-dev-2026` and are written as `path:line` or `path:start-end`. Paths in the second repo are prefixed with `oc-config/` (meaning `C:/Users/hp/Desktop/makun-ai-oc-config-master/`). Paths starting with `starlette/`, `celery/`, `fastapi/` and other package names in section 12 are relative to the scratch virtualenv's `site-packages/`.
6. Versions are "locked" when they come from `poetry.lock`. The scratch virtualenv is not a faithful install of the lock: 79 of the 179 locked packages are installed at another version and 8 are absent (verified by comparing every `[[package]]` block with the `*.dist-info` folder names). Every package whose source this map cites as "verified" is installed at its locked version, except asyncpg, pillow, livekit-api, prometheus-client and grpcio; section 12.3 says which claims that affects.
7. This document does not explain how to run the project. It describes what the code does as checked out, including the parts that do not import on this machine (section 1.5).
8. Acronyms are expanded the first time they are used in each section.

# 1. What the project does

## 1.1 Two repositories, one product

There are two repositories on this machine. They belong to the same product, a multi-tenant "AI agent" platform owned by MakunAI ("partners" are the tenants).

| Repo | Folder | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| `makun-ai` (branch `feature-dev-2026`) | `C:/Users/hp/Desktop/makun-ai-feature-dev-2026` | The service code: one Python package `src/` (494 non-test `.py` files, 90,519 lines, verified), 87 test files, 34 Alembic migrations, a `Dockerfile`, a `docker-compose.yml`, one Kubernetes manifest (`k8s/campaign-dispatch-worker.yaml`), two GitHub Actions workflows and the older company docs in `docs/architecture/`. `README.md` is empty (verified). |
| `makun-ai-oc-config` (branch `master`) | `C:/Users/hp/Desktop/makun-ai-oc-config-master` | GitOps configuration for the dev cluster: one ArgoCD `Application` (`argocd/apps/makun-ai-dev.yaml`) and one Kubernetes `Secret` (`dev/makun-ai-env-secret.yaml`) plus a lint workflow. It contains no application code. |

The service is called `makunai-service` in `pyproject.toml:2`. Its process name at runtime comes from the `SERVICE_NAME` environment variable (`src/core/environment.py:11`). Every HTTP route it serves is mounted under the prefix `/ai/v1/` (`src/routes/constants.py:1`, imported into each controller module and applied as `APIRouter(prefix=f"{BASE_URL_PREFIX}...")`, for example `src/components/agents/controllers.py:63` and `src/components/chat/controllers.py:41`; `src/routes/base.py:34` imports the constant but never uses it), except `GET /health` (`src/components/health/controller.py:125`) and `GET /robots.txt` (`src/components/security/robots.py:9`).

## 1.2 The product features

Everything below is a Python package under `src/components/`. The line counts are verified, non-test lines.

**Agents (4,318 lines).** A partner creates an "agent": a row in Postgres with a name, an LLM (Large Language Model) provider and model, a system prompt, assigned tools, allowed channels and voice settings. The `agents` package exposes CRUD (create/read/update/delete), publish and deactivate routes under `/ai/v1/agents`, keeps a history of system-prompt versions, manages parent-to-child "sub-agent routes" for delegation, and issues per-agent secrets for embedded widgets. At runtime it builds the `AgentConfig` object (cached in Redis) and the `LLMRequest` that every chat or voice turn sends to the model (`src/components/agents/services.py`, 1,209 lines).

**Text chat with SSE streaming and attachments (chat 4,712 lines; context 1,236; history_manager 294).** `POST` routes under `/ai/v1/chat` stream the agent's answer as Server-Sent Events (SSE), let a client approve or reject a paused tool call, and accept file uploads. The package enforces per-partner rate limits and per-session locks in Redis. The `context` package decides which history the LLM sees on each turn (Redis hot tier, MongoDB sliding window, compressed summaries, session facts, RAG chunks). The `history_manager` package reads and appends transcript messages in the MongoDB collection `makun_ai_chat_transcripts`. The chat service also converts answers into WhatsApp text formatting when the request interface is WhatsApp (`src/components/chat/services.py:684-702`).

**LLM loop and tool calling (llm 6,007 lines; orchestration 732; tools 5,920).** `src/components/llm/` turns an `LLMRequest` into a stream of typed events. `LLMProviderFactory` picks OpenAI, Anthropic, Groq or Gemini (`src/components/llm/providers/`). `LLMEngine` (`src/components/llm/engine.py`, 1,058 lines) runs a ReAct loop: it runs tool calls through `ToolExecutor` (`src/components/tools/executor.py`) and feeds results back to the model until a plain text answer comes out. The `orchestration` package runs a child agent's own mini loop in memory when a parent agent calls the `delegate_to_agent` tool. The `tools` package stores each tool's JSON schema and HTTP target in Postgres, validates the model's arguments with `jsonschema`, and sends the HTTP call through pooled `httpx` clients. Tool definitions are managed under `/ai/v1/tools` and `/ai/v1/agents/{agent_id}/tools` (`src/components/tools/controllers.py:36-44`) and partner-owned tool credentials under `/ai/v1/tools/credentials` (`src/components/tools/credentials/controllers.py:37`).

**OAuth and MCP integrations (integrations 9,276 lines).** Provider templates, encrypted per-partner OAuth2 or API-key credentials (AES-256-GCM through `CredentialVault`, `src/components/integrations/core/vault.py`), an OAuth2 authorization-code flow with PKCE (Proof Key for Code Exchange), Model Context Protocol (MCP) tool discovery, and auto-registration of `provider__action` tool rows onto agents. Routes live under `/ai/v1/integrations` and `/ai/v1/agents/{agent_id}/integrations`.

**Real-time voice (voice 22,719 lines, the largest package).** Two paths exist.
1. LiveKit WebRTC rooms. `POST /ai/v1/voice/token` and `POST /ai/v1/voice/sessions` (`src/components/voice/controllers.py:68,160`) create a billing row, create a LiveKit room whose metadata carries a JSON `RoomMetadata` blob, and mint a LiveKit JWT for the browser. A separate worker process (`src/components/voice/worker.py`) joins each room and runs `VoiceSessionHandler` (`src/components/voice/session/handler.py`, 1,188 lines): VAD (Voice Activity Detection) -> STT (Speech-To-Text) -> LLM -> TTS (Text-To-Speech), or a Gemini/OpenAI speech-to-speech session.
2. PSTN/SIP relay. `holler-service` (a sibling company service that owns the phone numbers and the SIP trunk) opens the WebSocket `/ai/v1/voice/stream/{agent_id}` (`src/components/voice/controllers.py:403`) carrying raw call audio. `WsRelaySession` (`src/components/voice/session/ws_relay_session.py`) runs the same pipeline inside the FastAPI process, with no LiveKit room. The WebSocket is protected only by an HMAC (Hash-based Message Authentication Code) ticket (`src/components/voice/platform/stream_token.py`), because none of the `BaseHTTPMiddleware` subclasses run for WebSocket scopes (verified, see the middleware note in section 3.2).

The `voice/transcript` sub-package (pure Python, no I/O) decides when a spoken turn is complete and parses dictated e-mail, phone, OTP (One-Time Password) and date values (`src/components/voice/transcript/`).

**Outbound voice campaign dialer (campaign 11,093 lines; cdr 320).** A partner uploads a lead list, optionally a WhatsApp fallback flow, and a campaign that ties an agent, lead lists, DIDs (Direct Inward Dialing numbers, owned by holler-service) and pacing rules together (`/ai/v1/voice-campaigns`, `/ai/v1/lead-lists`, `/ai/v1/fallback-flows`, `/ai/v1/voice-dnc`, `/ai/v1/dispositions`, `/ai/v1/contacts`). A Celery loop (`dispatch_coordinator` every 10 s, then `dispatch_campaign_tick` per campaign, `src/components/campaign/tasks.py:202,315`) pops due recipients from Redis sorted sets and POSTs to holler-service `/call` (`src/components/campaign/services/dispatch.py`, 1,335 lines). Call results come back on three inbound webhooks under `/ai/v1/voice/webhooks` (`src/components/campaign/controllers/webhooks.py:28,146,170`): `babbler-reply`, `holler-cdr` and `tata-dialer-cdr` (each described in section 4.4). The `cdr` package is the CDR (Call Detail Record) table and repository.

**WhatsApp.** There is no Meta/WhatsApp inbound webhook route in `src/` (grep verified). WhatsApp and RCS (Rich Communication Services) messages reach this service only through `babbler-service`, another sibling service, on two paths: conversational turns arrive as ordinary `POST /ai/v1/chat/message` calls with `interface=whatsapp` (`src/components/chat/services.py:684-689`), and campaign fallback replies arrive on `POST /ai/v1/voice/webhooks/babbler-reply` with an `X-Webhook-Secret` header compared to `BABBLER_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (`src/components/campaign/controllers/webhooks.py:32,48`; that route updates campaign recipients only and never enters the chat pipeline, see section 4.4). Outbound WhatsApp sends go to `BABBLER_API_BASE_URL` (`src/core/environment.py:24`). `docs/architecture/01_SYSTEM_OVERVIEW.md` lists a "WhatsApp Inbound Webhook" as done; that route does not exist. `src/components/chat/media/constants.py:73` keeps a Meta Graph API base URL for media download only.

**RAG knowledge base (rag 3,572 lines).** RAG means Retrieval-Augmented Generation. Partners upload PDF/DOCX/XLSX/XLS/CSV files under `/ai/v1/knowledge`. A Celery task parses them (PyMuPDF, openpyxl, csv), chunks them (LangChain `RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter`, 500 characters), embeds them with OpenAI `text-embedding-3-small` and stores 1536-dimension vectors in Postgres/pgvector (`src/components/rag/models.py:8`). Retrieval is a cosine search scoped by partner and agent.

**Skin analysis (skin_analysis 3,774 lines).** The `analyze_skin` internal tool (and an HTTP variant under `/ai/v1/skin-analysis`) crops a face with OpenCV, sends it to an OpenAI vision model for scores, draws an annotated image with Pillow, pulls RAG context, asks for product recommendations, and generates an "improved skin" image.

**Prompt builder (prompt_builder 4,329 lines).** Reusable system-prompt templates (platform seeds in `src/components/prompt_builder/constants.py`, 1,247 lines, upserted at app startup) and SSE-streamed, LLM-generated prompts under `/ai/v1/prompt-templates` and `/ai/v1/prompt-builder`.

**Partner configuration and rate limits (partner_config 2,012 lines; db_constants 1,276).** One row per partner (`makun_ai_partner_configs`) with chat rate limit, daily token limit, feature toggles and a `meta_config` JSON that can carry the partner's own provider API keys; the values are mirrored into Redis at startup (`src/components/partner_config/services.py:351`). The row is read and edited under `/ai/v1/partner/config` (`src/components/partner_config/controllers.py:32-34`). `db_constants` is a small admin feature for versioned DB-backed constants under `/ai/v1/constants`.

**Sessions (1,170 lines).** Operational state of every chat session in Postgres (`makun_ai_chat_sessions`: ACTIVE/IDLE/CLOSED) with list/history/clear routes under `/ai/v1/sessions`, aged by the Celery beat task `sweep_idle_sessions` (`src/components/sessions/tasks.py:20`).

## 1.3 Who calls the service

The HTTP surface is 146 route decorators plus 1 WebSocket across 28 `APIRouter` objects (AST-parsed, verified). Every HTTP request passes through `AuthMiddleware` (`src/middlewares/authentication.py:23`), which accepts three credentials in this order:

| Caller | Credential | How it is checked |
|---|---|---|
| Partner back-end applications | `API-KEY` header | Redis cache `api_key:<value>`, else a gRPC (Google Remote Procedure Call) call `validate_api_key` to the external console-service (`src/middlewares/authentication.py:150-159`; client in `src/grpc_client/client_services/api_key_service_client.py:18`). |
| Embedded web widgets | `X-Agent-Secret` header | SHA-256 hash lookup against the agent's stored secret in Postgres (`src/middlewares/authentication.py:196-220`, `src/components/agents/secret_utils.py`). The request is then scoped to that one `agent_id`. |
| The MakunAI console dashboard (human users) | `Authorization: Bearer <JWT>` | Redis cache `token:<jwt>` (5 minutes, `authentication.py:130`), else gRPC `validate_token` to console-service (`src/grpc_client/client_services/auth_service_client.py:21`). The gRPC host comes from `CONSOLE_GRPC_HOST`/`CONSOLE_GRPC_PORT` (`src/grpc_client/grpc_client.py:24-25`). The proto files are `src/protos/auth.proto` and `src/protos/api_key_service.proto`; generated code is in `src/pub/`. |
| `holler-service` (telephony) | shared secret | `POST /ai/v1/voice/webhooks/tata-dialer-cdr` is excluded from `AuthMiddleware` (`authentication.py:45`) and checks `X-Webhook-Secret` against `CDR_WEBHOOK_RELAY_SECRET` itself (`webhooks.py:174,193-200`). The WebSocket relay uses an HMAC stream token minted by `POST /ai/v1/voice/ws-ticket` (`src/components/voice/controllers.py:373`) or by holler-service itself when it holds the same `MAKUNAI_STREAM_SECRET` (`src/components/voice/platform/stream_token.py:3-9`). |
| `babbler-service` (WhatsApp/RCS) | shared secret | `POST /ai/v1/voice/webhooks/babbler-reply` is excluded from `AuthMiddleware` (`authentication.py:48`) and checks `X-Webhook-Secret` against `BABBLER_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (`webhooks.py:48`). Both webhook rows are described in full in section 4.4. |
| Nobody (unauthenticated) | none | Only `/docs`, `/openapi.json`, `/robots.txt`, the OAuth callback `/ai/v1/integrations/oauth/callback` and three legacy `/holler-service/...` paths are in `excluded_paths` (`authentication.py:34-49`). Note that `GET /health` is **not** excluded, so an unauthenticated probe gets 401. |

Outbound, the service calls: console-service (gRPC), holler-service (`HOLLER_SERVICE_BASE_URL`, calls and DID pools), babbler-service (`BABBLER_API_BASE_URL`), LiveKit server (`LIVEKIT_URL`), the LLM vendors (OpenAI, Anthropic, Groq, Google Gemini), the speech vendors (Deepgram, Cartesia, Sarvam, Gnani), DigitalOcean Spaces for file storage (`DO_ENDPOINT_URL`, `DO_SPACE_NAME`, `src/core/environment.py:49-52`) and a mail-gateway (`MAILMG_API_URL`, `src/core/environment.py:332`). All of these are configured by environment variable name only; see section 2 for the libraries.

## 1.4 The second repo: GitOps configuration

`makun-ai-oc-config-master` has six tracked files (verified with `find`). Its `README.md` only says `template-oc-config`.

- `argocd/apps/makun-ai-dev.yaml:3-31` is an ArgoCD `Application` named `makun-ai-dev`. ArgoCD is a tool that watches a Git repo and applies its Kubernetes YAML to a cluster. This one watches `path: dev` on branch `master` of `github.com/Makunaiglobal/makun-ai-oc-config.git` (`:12-15`) and applies it to namespace `maglo-integration` (`:18`) with `automated: prune: true, selfHeal: true` (`:20-22`). "Prune" means resources deleted from Git are deleted from the cluster; "selfHeal" means manual cluster edits are reverted.
- `dev/makun-ai-env-secret.yaml` is a Kubernetes `Secret` named `makun-ai-env-secret` in that namespace, written as plaintext `stringData` and committed to Git. It holds 56 keys (verified, `:8-63`). 47 of them are read through `ENV` in `src/core/environment.py`: 46 have the same name as an `ENV` attribute (`CONSOLE_GRPC_HOST`, `POSTGRES_*`, `MONGO_*`, `CACHE_*`, `LIVEKIT_*`, `DEEPGRAM_API_KEY`, `CARTESIA_API_KEY`, `SARVAM_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `DO_*`, `MAILMG_*`, ...) and the key `ENV` is read as `ENV.ENVIRONMENT` (`:54`). The other nine are: `CREDENTIAL_MASTER_KEY_V1` and `CREDENTIAL_MASTER_KEY_CURRENT_VERSION`, read via `os.environ` by the credential vault (`src/components/integrations/constants.py:110-111`, `src/components/integrations/core/static_methods.py:29`); `CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS` and `ALLOWED_HOSTS`, read by pydantic `LocalSettings` (`src/config/settings.py:21-22`); and `DB_CONNECTION_URL`, `GOOGLE_CALENDAR_CLIENT_ID`, `GOOGLE_CALENDAR_CLIENT_SECRET`, `GOOGLE_CALENDAR_REDIRECT_URI`, `VAULT_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, which nothing in `src/` reads (grep verified). The values are secrets and are not reproduced in this documentation.
- `.github/workflows/pr-validate.yaml:13-20` runs on pull requests to `master` that touch YAML: `yamllint -s .` (`:43`, rules in `.yamllint`) and `kubeconform -strict` v0.6.7 (`:45-80`), skipping the `Application` and `AppProject` kinds because they have no Kubernetes JSON schema (`:79`). It never touches a cluster (`:9`).

Nothing in this repo deploys the API, the Celery workers or the voice worker. The only Kubernetes workload manifest for the product is `k8s/campaign-dispatch-worker.yaml` in the main repo, and it references a `secretRef` named `makunai-service-env` (`:48,106`), an image `makunai-service:latest` (`:35,93`) and an env var `REDIS_ADDRESS` (`:70,128`), none of which exist in the oc-config Secret (verified).

## 1.5 Four facts to know before reading any code

1. `src/components/digital_assets` is a git submodule (`.gitmodules`, url `github.com/MakunaiGlobal/digital_assets.git`) that is **not checked out** here: the folder is empty. Sixteen non-test files import from it (15 under `src/` plus `alembic/env.py:17`, grep for `from`/`import` lines; `src/core/container.py:89,95` is one of them), and three more only mention the name (`alembic/versions/7badb275c08a_v1.py`, `alembic/versions/ab6f7d532d2a_v11.py`, `src/components/chat/media/schemas.py:7`), so `src/main.py`, the Celery app, the voice worker and Alembic all fail with `ModuleNotFoundError` in this working copy. The file-storage code (`StorageHelper`, `DigitalAssetService`, the `boto3` client for DigitalOcean Spaces) lives inside that submodule; `boto3` is declared in `pyproject.toml:48` but no file in `src/` imports it (grep verified).
2. `src/core/environment.py:1` has an unused `from curses.ascii import US`. On Windows this raises `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named '_curses'` and nothing in the service can import. It works on the Linux Docker image.
3. `src/core/environment.py:414-415` calls `ENV.validate_env_vars()` at import time whenever the environment variable `ENV` is non-empty. It raises `EnvironmentError` listing every required variable that is empty. This runs in every process type (API, Celery, voice worker, Alembic, scripts).
4. Two files start with a UTF-8 BOM (byte order mark): `src/components/common/pagination.py` and `src/components/sessions/controllers.py` (verified). Python tolerates it; some tools do not.

# 2. Tech stack with versions

All versions are "locked" in `poetry.lock` unless noted. The scratch virtualenv used for verification matches the lock for every package whose source this map cites, except asyncpg 0.31.0 (locked 0.30.0), pillow 11.3.0 (10.4.0), livekit-api 1.2.1 (1.2.0), prometheus-client 0.26.0 (0.25.0) and grpcio 1.84.0 (1.80.0); 79 locked packages in total are at another version and 8 are absent (section 12.3). `pyproject.toml` declares only `^` ranges; the lock file is the real version source and the `Dockerfile:12` copies it in as-is.

| Layer | Technology | Locked version | Where configured (file:line) | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Runtime | Python | `>=3.11,<3.15` (range, `pyproject.toml:8`) | `Dockerfile:1` image `python:3.11-slim-bookworm` | The interpreter. The image is Debian 12 with Python 3.11. |
| Runtime | Poetry | 2.3.3 | `Dockerfile:14-17` (`pip3 install poetry==2.3.3`, `poetry install --no-root --without local`) | Dependency manager that reads `pyproject.toml` and `poetry.lock`. |
| Runtime | Docker / docker-compose | compose file format `'3'` | `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml:3-167` (9 services) | Container build and local multi-process start. |
| Web | FastAPI | 0.115.14 | `src/main.py:27-31` | The HTTP framework; routes are `APIRouter`s registered in `src/routes/base.py:39`. |
| Web | Starlette | 0.46.2 | `src/middlewares/__init__.py:30-43` (`Middleware` list) | The ASGI toolkit under FastAPI; supplies the middleware classes and WebSocket support. |
| Web | uvicorn | 0.34.3 | `Dockerfile:23` (`uvicorn src.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 7000`) | The ASGI server process that runs the app. |
| Web | starlette-context | 0.3.6 | `src/middlewares/context.py:5`, `src/loggers/base.py:5` | Per-request context store (request id, auth info) readable from anywhere during a request. The only middleware that also runs for WebSockets (verified `starlette_context/middleware/raw_middleware.py:71-76`). |
| Web | python-multipart | 0.0.27 | `src/components/chat/media/controllers.py:2` (`UploadFile`) | Parser for `multipart/form-data` file uploads. |
| Web | pydantic / pydantic-settings | 2.13.4 / 2.13.1 | `src/config/settings.py:2-5` (`LocalSettings(BaseSettings)`) | Data validation for request/response schemas; settings loaded from env vars. |
| Web | python-dotenv | 1.2.2 | `src/core/environment.py:5,7`; `src/config/__init__.py:13` | Loads a `.env` file into `os.environ` (called twice, `override=False`). |
| DI | dependency-injector | 4.49.0 | `src/core/container.py:857` (`class Container(containers.DeclarativeContainer)`) | Dependency Injection container: one object that builds every repository/service lazily and patches `Provide[...]` markers into 27 modules on `Container()` (`container.py:895`). |
| ORM | SQLAlchemy (async) | 2.0.49 | `src/core/db.py:76` (`postgres_session_manager`), `src/main.py:39-53` | Object-Relational Mapper; all Postgres access uses `AsyncSession`. |
| DB driver | asyncpg | 0.30.0 | `src/main.py:40`, `alembic/env.py:68` (URL scheme `postgresql+asyncpg://`) | Async PostgreSQL driver used by SQLAlchemy. |
| DB driver | psycopg2-binary | 2.9.11 | `pyproject.toml:23` only; no import in `src/` or `alembic/` (grep verified) | Sync PostgreSQL driver. Declared but unused by project code. |
| ORM helper | sqlalchemy-utils | 0.41.2 | `src/components/db_constants/models.py:3` | Extra column types for SQLAlchemy. |
| Migrations | Alembic | 1.18.4 | `alembic.ini:4` (`script_location = alembic`), `alembic/env.py:10` | Schema migration tool; 34 linear revisions in `alembic/versions/`. |
| Database | PostgreSQL 16 + pgvector extension | image `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` | `docker-compose.yml:125`; host port 4000 -> 5432 (`:132`) | Main relational store (about 41 tables) plus vector similarity search. |
| Vector type | pgvector (Python) | 0.3.6 | `src/components/rag/models.py:8`; `alembic/versions/8aa63fb1d422_v5.py:12` | SQLAlchemy column type `Vector` for the 1536-d embeddings. |
| Database | MongoDB 6 | image `mongo:6` | `docker-compose.yml:110`; host port 27016 -> 27017 (`:117`) | Document store for chat transcripts (`makun_ai_chat_transcripts`) and summaries. |
| DB driver | motor | 3.7.1 | `src/core/db_mongo.py:3`, `src/core/doc_db.py:3` | Async MongoDB client (`AsyncIOMotorClient`). |

# 3. Entry points

## 3.1 Every process and command that starts the system

| Process / command | Exact command | Defined in | What it starts |
|---|---|---|---|
| HTTP API | `uvicorn src.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 7000` | `Dockerfile:23` (CMD); `docker-compose.yml:4-23` service `makunai-service` (also publishes port 50050, which nothing listens on) | The FastAPI app, all 28 routers, the WebSocket relay. Section 3.2. |
| Celery general worker | `poetry run celery -A src.makunai_celery.celery_app.celery worker --loglevel=info --concurrency=4` | `docker-compose.yml:25-41` service `celery-worker` | No `-Q`, so it consumes all four declared queues (verified `celery/app/amqp.py` `Queues.consume_from`). Section 3.3. |
| Celery campaign dispatch worker | same with `-Q campaign_dispatch_queue` | `docker-compose.yml:43-63`; `k8s/campaign-dispatch-worker.yaml:12-72` (2..100 replicas by KEDA) | Only `dispatch_campaign_tick`. |
| Celery post-call worker | same with `-Q campaign_post_call_queue` | `docker-compose.yml:65-81`; `k8s/campaign-dispatch-worker.yaml:74-130` (2..50 replicas) | Only `stale_dialing_sweep`. |
| Celery beat | `poetry run celery -A src.makunai_celery.celery_app.celery beat --loglevel=info` | `docker-compose.yml:83-96` service `celery-beat` | The scheduler that publishes the 8 `beat_schedule` entries (`src/makunai_celery/celery_config.py:49-104`). Must run exactly once. No Kubernetes manifest exists for it. |
| LiveKit voice worker | `poetry run python -m src.components.voice.worker start` | `docker-compose.yml:141-167` service `makunai-livekit-worker` (command `:147`) | The livekit-agents worker. Section 3.4. No Kubernetes manifest exists for it in either repo. |
| LiveKit voice worker, local variant | `poetry run python -m src.components.voice.worker_local dev` | `src/components/voice/worker_local.py:83-84`; commented out at `docker-compose.yml:146` | Same worker, but builds `RoomMetadata` from `TEST_*` env vars when the room has no metadata (`worker_local.py:17-40`), then calls the normal `entrypoint` (`:80`). No prewarm, no load function. |
| Alembic migrations | `alembic upgrade head` (and `downgrade`, `revision`) | `alembic.ini:4`; `alembic/env.py:119-122` | Applies the 34 revisions `v1` (`7badb275c08a`) to `v34` (`b3f1a9c4d2e7`). Section 3.5. |
| Model download at build time | `python -m livekit.agents download-files` | `Dockerfile:18` | Downloads the Silero/turn-detector model files into the image (verified `livekit/agents/__main__.py:104,131`). |
| Greeting pre-render script | `python scripts/synth_greeting.py --text "..." [--provider] [--voice] [--realtime-voice] [--out-dir]` | `scripts/synth_greeting.py:73-104` | Writes `<sha1>.mulaw` + `.json` sidecar into `assets/greetings/` (or `GREETING_CACHE_DIR`) using the TTS chain; the WebSocket relay plays these files without a TTS call. The only file in `scripts/`. |
| Skin-scan check script | `python -m src.components.skin_analysis.scripts.check_scans` | `src/components/skin_analysis/scripts/check_scans.py:1-21` (`__main__` at `:20`) | Builds a `Container()` and prints the last three `SkinScan` rows. Debug helper. |
| Skin simulation script | `python3 -m src.components.skin_analysis.scripts.simulate /path/to/image.jpg` | `src/components/skin_analysis/scripts/simulate.py:1-102` (`__main__` at `:101`) | Runs `GptImageSimulationEngine` directly with an `AsyncOpenAI` client. Debug helper. |
| CI build | push to `master` / `feature-dev-2026`, or any PR | `.github/workflows/build.yaml:3-7` | `poetry install --no-root` only; tests are commented out (`:49-63`). |
| CI deploy | push to `feature--dev--2026` | `.github/workflows/deployment.yaml:3-6` | Checks out submodules on branch `feature-dev-2025` (`:29`), builds the `Dockerfile`, pushes to the DigitalOcean registry (`:43-49`). |
| GitOps sync | push to `master` of `makun-ai-oc-config` | `oc-config/argocd/apps/makun-ai-dev.yaml` | ArgoCD applies `dev/makun-ai-env-secret.yaml` into `maglo-integration`. |

Every one of these Python processes imports `src.core.environment` first, which runs `load_dotenv()` (`src/core/environment.py:7`) and, when the env var `ENV` is set, `ENV.validate_env_vars()` (`:414-415`).

## 3.2 The API process: `src/main.py`

**At import time** (when uvicorn imports `src.main`), in this order:

1. `src/main.py:4-21` imports. These pull in `src.components.db_constants.initializer`, `src.core.db`, `src.core.container`, `src.middlewares` and `src.routes`. Importing `src.core.db` builds the module-level `postgres_session_manager` engine (`src/core/db.py:76`). Importing `src.core.db_mongo` builds the module-level `mongo_database_manager` Motor client (`src/core/db_mongo.py:31`). Importing `src.config` instantiates `LocalSettings()` (`src/config/settings.py:5,47-49`), which raises a pydantic `ValidationError` if the five `MONGO_*` variables are missing. Importing `src.middlewares` builds the six-entry middleware list (`src/middlewares/__init__.py:30-43`) and an `EmailService()` instance for `ErrorHandlingMiddleware`.
2. `src/main.py:24` `container = Container()`. Because `WiringConfiguration.auto_wire` defaults to `True`, constructing the container imports and patches the 27 modules listed in `src/core/container.py:857-895` (three middleware modules and 24 controller/repository modules). This is where the missing `digital_assets` submodule breaks the import (`container.py:89,95`).
3. `src/main.py:25` `container.check_dependencies()`. This is a no-op: `Container` overrides the method with `def check_dependencies(self): pass` (`src/core/container.py:1373-1374`), so the library check (`dependency_injector/containers.pyx:420-442`, which would walk all `providers.Dependency` providers and raise `errors.Error` for an undefined one) never runs. Nothing is validated and no object is created (see also 11.5).
4. `src/main.py:27-31` `app = FastAPI(title=ENV.SERVICE_NAME, container=container, middleware=allowed_middlewares)`. The unknown keyword `container=` lands in `app.extra`; the WebSocket handler reads it back as `websocket.app.extra["container"]` (`src/components/voice/controllers.py:421`) and passes it to `WsRelaySession` (`:446`). Starlette builds the stack as `ServerErrorMiddleware` -> user middleware in list order -> `ExceptionMiddleware` -> router (verified `starlette/applications.py:90-99`), so a request passes `ContextMiddleware`, `CORSMiddleware`, `AllowedHostsMiddleware`, `AuthMiddleware`, `ErrorHandlingMiddleware`, `RequestResponseLoggingMiddleware`, then the route. Four of those subclass `BaseHTTPMiddleware`, which forwards any non-`http` scope untouched (verified `starlette/middleware/base.py:98-101`), and CORS skips non-http too (`starlette/middleware/cors.py:76`), so only `ContextMiddleware` runs for the WebSocket route.
5. `src/main.py:33` `Router.register_all_routes(app)` (`src/routes/base.py:39-64`): calls `app.include_router(...)` once for each of the 25 component routers, in the fixed order of that file. There is no shared prefix router: `BASE_URL_PREFIX` is imported at `base.py:34` but unused there; every router sets its own `prefix=f"{BASE_URL_PREFIX}..."` (for example `src/components/voice/controllers.py:47`), and `health_router` has none (`src/components/health/controller.py:10`), so `GET /health` stays outside `/ai/v1/`.
6. `src/main.py:34` `SwaggerConfig.get_swagger_config(app)` (`src/config/swagger.py:9-16`): calls `app.openapi()`, which generates **and caches** the schema, then adds a `BearerAuth` security scheme and a global security requirement. The `API-KEY` and `X-Agent-Secret` headers are not documented there.
7. `src/main.py:35` `app.include_router(RobotsController.robot_router)` (`src/components/security/robots.py:7-9`). Because the schema was cached one line earlier, `/robots.txt` is absent from `/openapi.json`.
8. `src/main.py:37` `session_factory = MongoDatabaseManager().session`: a **third** Motor client, after the module-level `mongo_database_manager` from step 1 and the container's `DocDatabaseSessionManager` (`container.py:901`). It is imported only by `src/components/db_constants/loader/dynamic_variable.py:8`, a module nothing imports (grep verified; see 5.1).
9. `src/main.py:39-53` `sql_session_factory = DatabaseSessionManager("postgresql+asyncpg://...{ssl}", {"echo": settings.echo_sql}).session`: a **third** Postgres engine (after `postgres_session_manager` and the container's `db` provider at `container.py:900`). It appends `?ssl=require` unless `ENV.ENVIRONMENT` (the `ENV` variable, `src/core/environment.py:54`) equals the `Local` value. `echo_sql` defaults to `True` (`src/config/settings.py:6`), so every SQL statement on this engine is logged unless the env var overrides it. It is imported by `src/components/db_constants/loader/global_dynamic_variable.py:8`.

**`startup_event`** (`src/main.py:58-81`, registered with `@app.on_event("startup")`; `FastAPI.on_event` is marked deprecated in favour of lifespan handlers, verified `fastapi/applications.py:4476`):

1. `:60` `await container.init_resources()`: initialises the async `providers.Resource` objects, which creates the Redis client (`src/core/redis.py:19-30`, `container.py:903`), the pooled tool HTTP clients and the LLM provider factory cache.
2. `:62-63` opens a session on the container's `db` engine and runs `seed_agent_templates(session)` (`src/components/prompt_builder/template_initializer.py:135`): upserts the platform prompt templates from `prompt_builder/constants.py`.
3. `:65-69` `PartnerConfigService.sync_all_configs_to_redis()` (`src/components/partner_config/services.py:351`) inside `try/except`; a failure is only logged as a warning.
4. `:77` `ConstantsCreator.create_constants(sql_session_factory)` (`src/components/db_constants/initializer.py:132`) and `:78` `ConstantsInitializer.initialize_constants(sql_session_factory)` (`:47`), surrounded by `print()` banners. These use the third engine from import step 9. There is no `try/except`, so a failure here stops the app from starting.

**`shutdown_event`** (`src/main.py:84-87`): only `await postgres_session_manager.close()`. It does not call `container.shutdown_resources()`, so the Redis client created in step 1 and the container's own Postgres engine, the two Motor clients and the third engine are never closed explicitly.

## 3.3 The Celery processes: `src/makunai_celery/celery_app.py`

Importing `src.makunai_celery.celery_app` (what `celery -A ...celery_app.celery` does) builds this, in order:

1. `celery_app.py:4` imports `Container` (so the `digital_assets` import problem applies here too) and `:6` imports `celery_config`, which at import time computes `REDIS_URL = ENV.CACHE_DATABASE_URL()` (`celery_config.py:10`), parses `ENV.CHAT_SESSION_SWEEP_CRON` into five crontab fields with a fallback to `*/15 * * * *` (`:42-45`), and defines `task_routes` (`:18-31`), `task_default_queue = "default_queue"`, `task_acks_late = True`, `worker_prefetch_multiplier = 1`, `timezone = "UTC"` (`:33-39`) and `beat_schedule` (`:49-104`).
2. `celery_app.py:11` `_ssl_options = ENV.CACHE_SSL_OPTIONS() or None` (TLS only when `CACHE_PROTOCOL` is `rediss`).
3. `celery_app.py:13-19` `create_celery(...)` (`src/makunai_celery/celery_utils.py:16-46`): `Celery(app_name, broker=REDIS_URL, backend=REDIS_URL)`, optional `broker_use_ssl`/`redis_backend_use_ssl`, then `celery.autodiscover_tasks(["src.makunai_celery.tasks"])`. Verified in `celery/loaders/base.py:251-280`: autodiscover first imports `src.makunai_celery.tasks` itself (that import is the side effect that registers the tasks), then looks for `src.makunai_celery.tasks.tasks`, which does not exist and is ignored.
4. `celery_app.py:20` `celery.config_from_object("src.makunai_celery.celery_config")` applies the settings from step 1.
5. `celery_app.py:22-30` declares four `kombu.Queue`s: `default_queue`, `common_notifications_queue`, `campaign_dispatch_queue`, `campaign_post_call_queue`. No task is routed to `common_notifications_queue`.
6. `celery_app.py:32` `celery.container = Container()`: a full DI container attached to the Celery app, with the same auto-wiring import as the API. Several tasks ignore it and build their own `Container()` or `DatabaseSessionManager` (for example `src/components/campaign/tasks.py:178`).

`src/makunai_celery/tasks.py` defines no task; it imports 12 of the 14 task functions by name from `src.components.{campaign,context,integrations,orchestration,rag,sessions}.tasks` (`:11-18,36-49`; `__all__` at `:20-33` lists the same 12). The other two, `persist_session_fact` (`src/components/context/tasks.py:62`) and `scheduled_campaign_start` (`src/components/campaign/tasks.py:504`), are registered as a side effect of importing their modules, not by name; all 14 end up in the worker registry. Task names: a task's default name is `<module path>.<function>` (verified `celery/utils/imports.py:122` `gen_task_name`, called from `celery/app/base.py:600,650-651`), and `task_routes` matches exact names first and `*` patterns with `fnmatch` (verified `celery/app/routes.py:26-53`). Consequences, all verified: the pattern `"src.makunai_celery.tasks.*"` (`celery_config.py:19`) matches no real task; only `dispatch_campaign_tick` goes to `campaign_dispatch_queue` and only `stale_dialing_sweep` to `campaign_post_call_queue`; everything else, including `dispatch_placement_batch`, falls to `default_queue`. The beat entry `sweep-expiring-credentials` (`celery_config.py:99-103`) sends the name `src.components.integrations.tasks.sweep_expiring_credentials`, but the task is registered with the explicit name `sweep_expiring_credentials` (`src/components/integrations/tasks.py:23`), so every worker discards that message as unregistered and the credential sweep never runs. The complete per-task inventory (queue, trigger, retries, reads/writes) is the table in section 4.2.2; the routing and beat-name defects are also listed in section 11.9.

## 3.4 The LiveKit voice worker: `src/components/voice/worker.py`

`python -m src.components.voice.worker start` runs the module as `__main__`. The `start` subcommand runs the worker in production mode; `dev` sets `devmode=True` (verified `livekit/agents/cli/cli.py:307`). Four phases happen, in different processes.

**Phase A, import time (orchestrator process, `worker.py:1-161`).**
- `:13-16` sets `OMP_NUM_THREADS`, `ONNXRUNTIME_NUM_THREADS`, `MKL_NUM_THREADS`, `NUMEXPR_NUM_THREADS` to `1`.
- `:18-20` imports `livekit.agents` and `ENV`.
- `:63-71` `_preload_plugin(...)` imports `livekit.plugins.cartesia`, `.deepgram`, `.openai`, `.silero`, `.google` (only if `GEMINI_API_KEY`), `.gnani` (only if `GNANI_API_KEY`, which is always true because the source has a non-empty default), `.dtln`. Each import is wrapped in `try/except: pass` (`:56-60`), so a missing plugin is silently skipped.
- `:94` preloads `livekit.plugins.turn_detector.multilingual` so the framework starts its shared inference subprocess.
- `:125` preloads `src.components.voice.session.handler` (which drags in the whole pipeline and `google.genai`).
- `:139-140` preloads `langchain_openai` if `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set.
- `:158` installs a logging filter that downgrades a known benign LiveKit error to INFO.
- `:161-163` `MAX_ACTIVE_CALLS = 6`, `_container = None`, `_container_lock = asyncio.Lock()`.

**Phase B, `__main__` (`worker.py:754-773`).** `cli.run_app(WorkerOptions(...))` (verified `livekit/agents/cli/cli.py:471`; field definitions in `livekit/agents/worker.py:181-269`) with: `entrypoint_fnc=entrypoint`, `prewarm_fnc=prewarm_process`, `num_idle_processes=1`, `initialize_process_timeout=20.0`, `load_fnc=worker_load`, `load_threshold=0.99`, `prometheus_port=ENV.VOICE_WORKER_METRICS_PORT` (default 9090, `src/core/environment.py:14`), `prometheus_multiproc_dir="/tmp/prometheus_multiproc_voice_worker"`. The framework connects to `LIVEKIT_URL` with `LIVEKIT_API_KEY`/`LIVEKIT_API_SECRET`, keeps one idle job process warm, and exposes `GET /metrics` with no auth. `worker_load` (`:531-549`) runs every 0.5 s in the main process and returns `min(len(worker.active_jobs) / MAX_ACTIVE_CALLS, 1.0)` with `MAX_ACTIVE_CALLS = 6` (`:161`), so with `load_threshold=0.99` the LiveKit dispatcher stops sending calls to this pod at 6 concurrent calls.

**Phase C, `prewarm_process` (`worker.py:249-515`), once per forked job process.**
- `:283-292` timed re-imports of the same plugins on the main thread (plugin registration is main-thread bound).
- `:316-336` pre-builds the shared Gemini, Groq and OpenAI LLM clients, each gated on its API key.
- `:363` defines `_bg_warmup` and `:512-513` starts it as a daemon thread, then `:515` sets `proc.userdata["warmup_started"] = True` and returns, so the process reports "ready" quickly. Inside the thread: `:382` `proc.userdata["vad"] = silero.VAD.load()`; `:410` `google.genai` `LiveConnectConfig.model_rebuild()`; `:431` builds the shared `SSLContext`; `:441-465` raw TCP/TLS handshakes to `api.groq.com`, `api.openai.com`, `generativelanguage.googleapis.com`, `api.cartesia.ai`, `api.deepgram.com` (`raw_tcp_warmup`, `:187-197`); `:487` `_container = Container()` (the DI container for this job process); `:494` `warm_sqlalchemy_mappers(logger)` imports the eight model modules listed at `:171-180` and calls `configure_mappers()`; `:500` `gc.freeze()`.

**Phase D, `entrypoint(ctx)` (`worker.py:716-751`), once per call.**
1. `:720` `await ctx.connect(auto_subscribe=AutoSubscribe.AUDIO_ONLY)`: joins the room before any heavy work so the first words are not lost.
2. `:722-723` classifies the call as `pstn` or `web` with `_is_pstn_call` (`:694-713`: room name starting with `call-`, or metadata with `caller_phone` or a `CAGE...` session id).
3. `:726` `container = await get_container_async()` (`:518-528`): waits in 50 ms steps until `_bg_warmup` has set `_container`.
4. `:736` `await _run_backend_warmups(...)` (`:552-691`): awaits Postgres and LLM-factory warmups, fires Mongo, tool-client and Gemini warmups as background tasks; each is guarded by a flag in `ctx.proc.userdata` so it runs once per process.
5. `:740-745` `VoiceSessionHandler(ctx=ctx, container=container)`, then `await session.setup()` and `await session.run()`. Everything after this point is in `src/components/voice/session/`.

## 3.5 Alembic: `alembic/env.py`

`alembic.ini:4` points `script_location` at `alembic/`; the `sqlalchemy.url` placeholder at `alembic.ini:8` is overwritten at runtime. `alembic/env.py` does, in order: `:13` inserts the repo root into `sys.path`; `:15-60` imports every ORM model module so `Base.metadata` is complete for autogenerate (this includes the missing `src.components.digital_assets.models` at `:17`); `:68-78` builds `postgresql+asyncpg://...` from `ENV.POSTGRES_*` and sets it as `sqlalchemy.url` (no `?ssl=require`, unlike `src/main.py:47`); `:83` `target_metadata = Base.metadata` (`src/database/base.py`); `:119-122` picks offline mode (`run_migrations_offline`, `:86-95`, emits SQL with literal binds) or online mode (`run_migrations_online`, `:115-116`, which runs `asyncio.run(run_async_migrations())`, `:104-112`: `async_engine_from_config(..., poolclass=NullPool)`, `connection.run_sync(do_run_migrations)`, `:98-101` `context.configure` + `begin_transaction` + `run_migrations`). The revision chain is linear, `v1` to `v34` (verified).

## 3.6 One-off scripts

- `scripts/synth_greeting.py:73-104`: `argparse` with `--text` (required), `--provider`, `--voice`, `--realtime-voice`, `--out-dir`; `asyncio.run(_synthesize(...))` walks the TTS chain (Sarvam then OpenAI per the docstring, `:13-14`) and raises `RuntimeError("all TTS providers failed")` when every step fails (`:70`); writes raw 8 kHz mono mu-law bytes plus a JSON sidecar whose `voices` label must match `WsRelaySession._greeting_voices_label` or the relay ignores the file (`:8-11`).
- `src/components/skin_analysis/scripts/check_scans.py` (21 lines) and `simulate.py` (102 lines): developer debug scripts, see the table in 3.1. Neither is referenced by any Dockerfile, compose service or workflow.

# 4. How things enter and leave the system

This section follows one unit of work through each entry point of the service: an HTTP request, a Celery job, a voice call, an inbound webhook, an outbound call to another company service, and a streamed chat reply. Every path name is relative to the repo root `C:/Users/hp/Desktop/makun-ai-feature-dev-2026`. Secrets are named by variable name only.

## 4.1 HTTP request lifecycle

### 4.1.1 Middleware runtime order (verified)

`src/main.py:27-31` builds the FastAPI app with `middleware=allowed_middlewares`. That list is defined in `src/middlewares/__init__.py:30-43`. Starlette builds the stack in `build_middleware_stack` (scratch venv `starlette/applications.py:90-99`, starlette 0.46.2 locked): `ServerErrorMiddleware` is outermost, then the user list in list order, then `ExceptionMiddleware`, then the router. So an incoming request passes through these layers in this exact order:

| # | Class | Defined in | Base class |
|---|-------|-----------|------------|
| 1 | `ContextMiddleware` | `src/middlewares/context.py:32` | `starlette_context` `RawContextMiddleware` |
| 2 | `CORSMiddleware` | FastAPI/Starlette, configured at `src/middlewares/__init__.py:32-38` | pure ASGI |
| 3 | `AllowedHostsMiddleware` | `src/middlewares/allowed_host.py:17` | `BaseHTTPMiddleware` |
| 4 | `AuthMiddleware` | `src/middlewares/authentication.py:23` | `BaseHTTPMiddleware` |
| 5 | `ErrorHandlingMiddleware` | `src/middlewares/exception.py:30` | `BaseHTTPMiddleware` |
| 6 | `RequestResponseLoggingMiddleware` | `src/middlewares/logging_request_and_response.py:15` | `BaseHTTPMiddleware` |
| 7 | the route handler | registered by `src/routes/base.py:39-64` | - |

Layers 3 to 6 subclass `BaseHTTPMiddleware`, which passes every non-`http` scope straight through (`starlette/middleware/base.py:98-101`), and `CORSMiddleware` also skips non-`http` scopes (`starlette/middleware/cors.py:76`). Only layer 1 handles both `http` and `websocket` (`starlette_context/middleware/raw_middleware.py:71-76`). The consequence for the one WebSocket route is described in 4.3.

**1. ContextMiddleware** (`src/middlewares/context.py:32-43`). It installs the `RequestIdPlugin` from `starlette_context`, copies the generated request id into the `ContextVar` `context_request_id_var` (`:10`) and into `request.state.request_id` (`:42`). The same module also owns a second `ContextVar`, `_request_auth` (`:19`), with `set_request_auth` (`:24`) and `get_request_auth` (`:28`). `AuthMiddleware` fills it with the caller's own credential header, and outbound HTTP clients read it later to forward that header (see 4.5).

**2. CORSMiddleware**. Cross-Origin Resource Sharing. Origins come from `local_settings.cors_origins_list()` (`src/middlewares/__init__.py:27`); `allow_credentials=True`, and all methods and headers are allowed (`:24-25,32-38`).

**3. AllowedHostsMiddleware** (`src/middlewares/allowed_host.py:23-58`). If the `content-type` header is exactly `application/json`, it first calls `await request.json()` (`:36-37`). This is a pre-read: malformed JSON is caught as `json.JSONDecodeError` and answered with `BadRequestResponse("Invalid JSON format")` (`:53-54`) before authentication runs. The body is not lost for later layers, because Starlette's `_CachedRequest.wrapped_receive` replays a body that `body()` already consumed (`starlette/middleware/base.py:59-62`, verified). Then it compares the `host` header, with and without port, against `allowed_hosts` from `local_settings.allowed_hosts_list()` (`:39-49`); a `*` entry disables the check (`:44-45`). A mismatch returns `ForbiddenResponse("Host Not Allowed")` (403).

**4. AuthMiddleware** (`src/middlewares/authentication.py:26-138`). The `excluded_paths` tuple is, verbatim (`:34-49`):

```
"/docs",
"/openapi.json",
"/robots.txt",
"/holler-service/v1/call/webhook",
"/holler-service/v1/call/api/dialplan",
"/holler-service/v1/reports/daily-lead-connection-csv",
"/ai/v1/integrations/oauth/callback",
"/ai/v1/voice/webhooks/tata-dialer-cdr",
"/ai/v1/voice/webhooks/babbler-reply",
```

The three `/holler-service/...` entries do not match any route in this service (no router uses that prefix, see the router list in `src/routes/base.py`); they are leftovers. The match is exact on `request.url.path` (`:54`). For every other path the middleware picks one of three branches, in this priority:

- **`API-KEY` header** (`:57-67`, handler `_handle_api_key` `:140-194`). It reads Redis key `api_key:{key}` (`:150`). On a miss it calls console-service over gRPC: `RPCServiceFactory.get_service(GrpcServices.API_KEY).validate_api_key(api_key)` (`:157-158`). An empty result returns 401 "Invalid API Key"; `is_active=False` returns 401 "Inactive API Key" (`:160-172`). It then sets `request.state.user = {"partner_id", "api_key_id", "is_api_key_auth": True}` (`:179-183`) and records the header in the auth `ContextVar` (`:188`). Note: this branch never writes the Redis cache (there is no `setex` between `:140` and `:194`), so the `api_key:{key}` read is a cache hit only if something else wrote it; every API-KEY request therefore does a gRPC round trip.
- **`X-Agent-Secret` header** (`:69-79`, handler `_handle_agent_secret` `:196-234`). It hashes the header with `hash_agent_secret` (`src/components/agents/secret_utils.py`) and looks the hash up with `AgentRepository.find_active_secret_by_hash(secret_hash, now_ms)` (`:205-209`), which reads the PostgreSQL table `makun_ai_agent_secrets` (`src/database/constants.py:18`, model at `src/components/agents/models.py:230`). No match returns 401. On a match `request.state.user = {"partner_id", "agent_id", "is_agent_secret_auth": True}` (`:217-221`); both ids come from the matched row, never from the caller.
- **`Authorization: Bearer <token>`** (`:82-138`). Missing or malformed header returns 401 (`:85-100`). It reads Redis key `token:{token}` (`:105`). A cached JSON payload with `user_id` and `partner_id` is used directly if `is_active` is true (`:111-115`). Otherwise it calls `AuthServiceClient.validate_token(token)` (`:118`), then `get_user_child_hierarchy(user_id)` (`:124`), stores the payload in `request.state.user` and the hierarchy in `request.state.hierarchy` (`:126-127`), and caches the payload for 300 seconds with `setex` (`:130`). Only this branch sets `user_id`; `require_console_auth` in `src/components/common/request_identity.py:60-97` relies on that to allow console-only endpoints.

Any exception inside a branch returns `InternalServerErrorResponse` (`:134-136,190-192,230-232`). The gRPC clients are described in 4.5.

**5. ErrorHandlingMiddleware** (`src/middlewares/exception.py:209-281`). It is constructed with `EmailService()` (`src/middlewares/__init__.py:41`). After `call_next`, if the status is 500 or higher it tries to send an error e-mail (`:231-248`). If `call_next` raises, it logs the traceback, sends the e-mail, and returns a plain-text 500 whose body includes `str(e)` (`:252-273`); in practice that branch is reached only for the paths that layer 6 skips, because layer 6 catches route exceptions first (see 4.1.5). Recipients are chosen by matching the request URL prefix against `ENVIRONMENT_CONFIG` (`:16-25`): the DEV entry matches `https://int-makun-ai-service.makunaiglobal.ai`, the PROD entry `https://makun-ai-service.makunaiglobal.ai`; any other host (for example `localhost`) yields no recipients and no e-mail (`:75-85`). The e-mail is built by `_prepare_error_email_data` (`:90-164`) and sent with template `error_mail.html` (`:197-202`). One defect: `:113` does `await RPCServiceFactory.get_service(...)`, but `get_service` is a plain function returning a client object (`src/grpc_client/rpc_service_factory.py:16-25`), so the `await` raises `TypeError`; the `except` at `:149` swallows it and the e-mail falls back to the reduced dictionary without user name.

**6. RequestResponseLoggingMiddleware** (`src/middlewares/logging_request_and_response.py:17-140`). It skips `/docs`, `/openapi.json`, `/robots.txt` and four `/holler-service/...` and `/reports/...` prefixes (`:26-40`). It reads the whole request body (`:43`) and logs method, URL, **all request headers** and the parsed body as one JSON line (`:62-71`); that includes the `Authorization`, `API-KEY` and `X-Agent-Secret` header values. For the response: 204 is passed through as an empty response (`:77-79`); a `StreamingResponse`, `text/event-stream` or any non-JSON content type is logged by status only and returned untouched (`:82-98`); a JSON response body is fully drained (`:101`), logged, and re-emitted as a new `StreamingResponse` with the same status and headers (`:124-132`). The whole `call_next` block sits in `try/except Exception` (`:73-75,134-140`): an exception raised by the route is logged and answered with the JSON `InternalServerErrorResponse()` (status 500), so layer 5 sees a 500 status, not an exception.

After the route returns, the response passes back through the same layers in reverse.

### 4.1.2 Dependency injection

`src/core/container.py:857` declares `class Container(containers.DeclarativeContainer)` from `dependency-injector` 4.49.0 (locked). `wiring_config` (`:895`) is a `WiringConfiguration` whose `modules` list (`:863-893`) names three middleware modules and every controller module (24 entries). `src/main.py:24` creates `container = Container()`; constructing it wires those modules, which is why the controller functions can declare parameters such as `service: AgentService = Depends(Provide[Container.agent_service])` under an `@inject` decorator. `src/main.py:29` also passes `container=container` into `FastAPI(...)`, which lands in `app.extra["container"]`; the WebSocket handler reads it from there (`src/components/voice/controllers.py:421`).

Provider types matter for lifetime: `logger` (`:897`), `db` (`:900`), `mongo_db` (`:901`), `agent_repository` (`:924`) and most repositories are `providers.Singleton`; services such as `agent_service` (`:927`) are `providers.Factory`, so a new service object is built per request; `redis_pool` (`:903`) is a `providers.Resource` whose async initialiser runs once in `startup_event` via `container.init_resources()` (`src/main.py:60`). Startup also seeds agent templates (`:62-63`), syncs partner configs to Redis (`:66-67`) and creates DB constants (`:77-78`). `@app.on_event` (`:58,84`) is the deprecated FastAPI hook; `fastapi/applications.py:4476` (0.115.14 locked) marks it deprecated in favour of lifespan handlers.

### 4.1.3 Controller -> service -> repository -> model, traced for `POST /ai/v1/agents`

1. **Router.** `agents_router = APIRouter(prefix=f"{BASE_URL_PREFIX}agents", tags=["Agents"])` (`src/components/agents/controllers.py:63`), included by `src/routes/base.py:43`.
2. **Controller.** `@agents_router.post("", response_model=AgentRead, status_code=201)` (`:266-276`) on `create_agent(request, payload: AgentCreate, service=Depends(Provide[Container.agent_service]), logger=...)` (`:278-283`). It reads identity with `AgentController.get_partner_id(request)` and `get_user_id(request)` (`:286-287`), calls `await service.create_agent(partner_id, user_id, payload)` (`:291`) and returns `ResourceCreatedResponse(data=agent, message="Agent created successfully")` (`:292-294`). `AgentAlreadyExistsError` becomes `BadRequestResponse(detail=str(exc))` (`:297-299`); any other exception becomes `InternalServerErrorResponse(detail="Failed to create agent.")` (`:300-302`).
3. **Identity helper.** `get_partner_id` lives in `src/components/common/request_identity.py:4-30`. It reads `request.state.user` (set by `AuthMiddleware`), raises `HTTPException(401)` when `partner_id` is absent and `strict=True`, and returns `int(user["partner_id"])`. `get_user_id` (`:33-57`) does the same for `user_id`. Controllers across the codebase call these two helpers rather than touching `request.state` directly; the voice and campaign controllers import them the same way (`src/components/voice/controllers.py`, `src/components/campaign/controllers/webhooks.py:15`).
4. **Service.** `AgentService.create_agent` (`src/components/agents/services.py:576-601`) folds `voice_mode_config` into `agent_config` (`:593-598`), calls `await self._repo.create_agent(partner_id, user_id, data)` (`:600`) and converts the ORM row with `_to_agent_read` (`:601`).
5. **Repository.** `AgentRepository.create_agent` (`src/components/agents/repositories.py:189-246`) opens `async with self.__session_factory() as session` (`:193`), runs `select(Agent).where(Agent.partner_id == partner_id, Agent.name == data.name)` (`:197-202`) and raises `AgentAlreadyExistsError` on a duplicate (`:203-204`), builds `Agent(...)` (`:206-224`) plus child `Tool(...)` rows (`:227-237`), then `session.add(agent)`, `flush()`, `refresh()`, `commit()` (`:239-242`).
6. **Model.** `class Agent(MetaData)` with `__tablename__ = AGENT_TABLE_NAME` (`src/components/agents/models.py:35,45`), where `AGENT_TABLE_NAME = "makun_ai_agents"` (`src/database/constants.py:1`).

### 4.1.4 Response envelope classes

All JSON responses are built from the classes in `src/components/common/responses.py`. `SuccessResponse` (`:55-81`) renders `{"status": "success", "message": ..., "data": ...}` with a default status code of 200; `data` passes through `jsonable_encoder`. The others fix their status code:

| Class | Line | HTTP status |
|-------|------|-------------|
| `SuccessResponse` | `:55` | 200 (parameter) |
| `ResourceCreatedResponse` | `:243` | 201 |
| `AcceptedResponse` | `:314` | 202 |
| `SuccessNoContentResponse` | `:267` | 204 |
| `BadRequestResponse`, `ValidationErrorResponse`, `InvalidFileFormat` | `:176`, `:83`, `:338` | 400 |
| `UnauthorizedResponse` | `:107` | 401 |
| `ForbiddenResponse`, `ForbiddenPermissionResponse` | `:220`, `:197` | 403 |
| `NotFoundResponse`, `ResourceNotFoundResponse` | `:130`, `:361` | 404 |
| `ResourceConflictResponse` | `:291` | 409 |
| `TooManyRequestsResponse` | `:384` | 429 |
| `InternalServerErrorResponse` | `:153` | 500 |
| `ChatStreamResponse` (SSE, see 4.6) | `:425` | 200 |

Lines `:273` and `:429` of this file contain example decorators inside docstrings; they are not routes.

### 4.1.5 How exceptions become HTTP errors

There is no global exception-to-response mapping. Each component defines plain exception classes in its own `errors.py` (for example `AgentException` and twelve subclasses such as `AgentNotFoundError`, `AgentAlreadyExistsError`, `AgentLimitExceededError` in `src/components/agents/errors.py:1-121`; `VoiceException`, `AgentNotReadyError`, `LiveKitConnectionError`, `SessionNotFoundError` in `src/components/voice/errors.py`). Services and repositories raise them; each controller handler wraps its body in `try/except` and maps each class to one of the response classes above, as in the agent example (`controllers.py:296-302`) and in the voice token handler (`src/components/voice/controllers.py:139-158`, 404 for `AgentNotReadyError`, 500 for `LiveKitConnectionError`, 400 for `VoiceException`). `HTTPException` is re-raised so FastAPI's default handler answers it (`controllers.py:295-296`). Anything a controller does not catch is re-raised by Starlette inside `call_next` (`starlette/middleware/base.py:152-156`; `ExceptionMiddleware` has no handler for it, `starlette/_exception_handler.py:52-53`) and is caught first by `RequestResponseLoggingMiddleware` (`src/middlewares/logging_request_and_response.py:134-140`), which returns the JSON `InternalServerErrorResponse()` (status 500). `ErrorHandlingMiddleware` then sees the 500 and only sends the error e-mail (`src/middlewares/exception.py:231-248`); its plain-text 500 body (`:269-273`) is produced only for the paths the logging middleware skips (`logging_request_and_response.py:26-40`). `src/exceptions/validation.py:9-17` defines a custom `RequestValidationError` handler, but no module calls `handler(app)` (grep verified) and it imports `src.components.common.response`, a module that does not exist (`:6`); request validation errors therefore use FastAPI's built-in 422 response.

## 4.2 Background job lifecycle

### 4.2.1 Path of one job

1. **Producer.** Either `celery-beat` (a separate process running `celery -A src.makunai_celery.celery_app.celery beat`, `docker-compose.yml:83-86`) reads `beat_schedule` (`src/makunai_celery/celery_config.py:49-104`), or application code calls `<task>.delay(...)`.
2. **Broker.** The Celery app is built by `create_celery` (`src/makunai_celery/celery_utils.py:16-46`) with `broker=backend=ENV.CACHE_DATABASE_URL()` (`celery_config.py:10`, `celery_app.py:13-19`), that is, the same Redis instance the API uses for caching; `CACHE_SSL_OPTIONS()` switches TLS on when the protocol is `rediss` (`celery_app.py:9-11`).
3. **Queue.** `task_routes` (`celery_config.py:18-31`) maps `dispatch_coordinator` to `default_queue`, `dispatch_campaign_tick` to `campaign_dispatch_queue` and `stale_dialing_sweep` to `campaign_post_call_queue`. The glob `"src.makunai_celery.tasks.*"` (`:19`) matches nothing, because no task is defined in that module (verified against `celery/app/routes.py:26-53` and `celery/utils/imports.py:122` `gen_task_name`, called from `celery/app/base.py:600,650-651`, celery 5.6.3 locked: a task's default name is `<module>.<function>`, and all tasks are defined under `src.components.*`). Everything else lands on `task_default_queue = "default_queue"` (`:33`). The four queues are declared in `celery_app.py:22-30`; `common_notifications_queue` has no producer in `src/` (grep verified).
4. **Worker pool.** `task_acks_late = True` and `worker_prefetch_multiplier = 1` (`celery_config.py:34-35`) mean a worker acknowledges a message only after the task finishes and takes one message at a time.
5. **Task registration.** `celery.autodiscover_tasks(["src.makunai_celery.tasks"])` (`celery_utils.py:42`) imports `src/makunai_celery/tasks.py`, which only re-imports the six component task modules (`:8-49`; 12 of the 14 tasks by name, the other two as import side effects, see 3.3) so their `@celery.task` / `@shared_task` decorators run inside the worker. `celery_app.py:32` attaches `celery.container = Container()` so tasks can resolve repositories and services.
6. **Task body -> stores.** Each task writes to PostgreSQL through SQLAlchemy sessions, to MongoDB through Motor, and to Redis; the table below lists them.

### 4.2.2 Job inventory (14 tasks)

Source: FACTS, `src/makunai_celery/celery_config.py`, the task files, and `scratchpad/surveys/celery-jobs.json`. "Queue" is where the message actually lands.

| Task name (registered) | file:line | Queue | Trigger | Retries / time limits | Reads / writes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fallback_advance_sweep` | `src/components/campaign/tasks.py:124` | `default_queue` | beat `fallback_advance_sweep`, every 15 min (`celery_config.py:60-63`) | `bind=True, max_retries=3, soft_time_limit=30, time_limit=45`, retry countdown 60 s | Reads recipients in status `FALLBACK_SENT`, campaign and fallback-flow nodes (PostgreSQL); calls `FallbackEngine.trigger_fallback` (`:113`) which sends the next WhatsApp/RCS node and updates recipient status |
| `sweep_live_sync_lead_lists` | `src/components/campaign/tasks.py:164` (`@shared_task` + `@inject`) | `default_queue` | beat, every 15 min (`:64-67`) | no `bind`, exceptions caught and logged (`:193-198`), no retry | Builds its own `Container()` (`:178`); reads lead lists with `sync_mode == live_sync`; calls `LeadListService.sync_lead_list`, which needs a user Bearer token from the request context and therefore fails inside a worker (survey `celery-jobs.json`, not re-verified here) |
| `src.components.campaign.tasks.dispatch_coordinator` | `src/components/campaign/tasks.py:202` | `default_queue` (`celery_config.py:20,86`) | beat `campaign-dispatch-coordinator`, every `DISPATCH_TICK_INTERVAL_SECONDS` = 10 s (`celery_config.py:79-87`, `campaign/constants.py:213`) | `bind=True, max_retries=3, soft_time_limit=20, time_limit=30` | Reads Redis sorted set `campaign:ready_set`, reconciles it from running campaigns in PostgreSQL, acquires a per-campaign Redis lease, then enqueues `dispatch_campaign_tick.delay(campaign_id, lease_pre_acquired=True)` (`:301`) |
| `src.components.campaign.tasks.dispatch_campaign_tick` | `src/components/campaign/tasks.py:315` | `campaign_dispatch_queue` (`celery_config.py:21-23`) | only `dispatch_coordinator` via `.delay` at `campaign/tasks.py:301` | `bind=True, max_retries=2, soft_time_limit=25, time_limit=40` | Reads campaign row, DID pool from holler-service (HTTP), Redis capacity counters; pops due recipients; `POST {HOLLER_SERVICE_BASE_URL}/call` per recipient (`campaign/services/dispatch.py:644,1086`); writes recipient status `DIALING` and attempts (PostgreSQL); releases the lease |
| `src.components.campaign.tasks.dispatch_placement_batch` | `src/components/campaign/tasks.py:408` | `default_queue` (no route; comment at `celery_config.py:24-27`) | `.delay` at `campaign/tasks.py:365`, reachable only when `USE_TWO_STAGE_DISPATCH` is true; it is hard-coded `False` (`campaign/constants.py:200`), so never enqueued | `bind=True, max_retries=2, soft_time_limit=25, time_limit=40` | Would place one batch of calls through `DispatchService.execute_placement_batch` |
| `src.components.campaign.tasks.stale_dialing_sweep` | `src/components/campaign/tasks.py:460` | `campaign_post_call_queue` (`celery_config.py:28-30,92`) | beat `campaign-stale-dialing-sweep`, every `STALE_SWEEP_INTERVAL_SECONDS` = 60 s (`:88-93`, `constants.py:227`) | `bind=True, max_retries=3, soft_time_limit=30, time_limit=45` | Reads recipients stuck in `DIALING` longer than `STALE_DIALING_THRESHOLD_SECONDS` = 150 s (`constants.py:215`); applies a system-error disposition or fallback; resyncs Redis in-flight counters |
| `src.components.campaign.tasks.scheduled_campaign_start` | `src/components/campaign/tasks.py:504` | `default_queue` (`celery_config.py:97`) | beat `campaign-scheduled-start`, every 60 s (`:94-98`) | `bind=True, max_retries=3, soft_time_limit=30, time_limit=45` | Reads published campaigns whose `scheduled_at` has passed (PostgreSQL); calls `CampaignService.start_campaign` |
| `src.components.context.tasks.compress_session` | `src/components/context/tasks.py:10` | `default_queue` | `compress_session.delay` from `src/components/history_manager/manager.py:157-159`, only when `ENV.COMPRESSION_ENABLED` | `bind=True, max_retries=3, default_retry_delay=60` | Reads uncompressed turns from the MongoDB transcript, calls `CompressionService` (an LLM call), inserts a summary block and marks turns compressed (MongoDB) |
| `src.components.context.tasks.persist_session_fact` | `src/components/context/tasks.py:62` | `default_queue` | `persist_session_fact.delay` from `src/components/context/session_memory.py:147`, flag-gated by `ENV.PERSIST_SESSION_FACT_ENABLED` | `bind=True, max_retries=3, default_retry_delay=10` | Upserts one `session_memory.{key}` fact into the MongoDB transcript via `ChatTranscriptRepository.upsert_session_fact` |
| `sweep_expiring_credentials` | `src/components/integrations/tasks.py:23` | `default_queue` | beat `sweep-expiring-credentials`, every 15 min (`celery_config.py:99-103`), **but** the beat entry names the task `src.components.integrations.tasks.sweep_expiring_credentials` while the decorator registers `sweep_expiring_credentials` (`:23`); beat falls back to `send_task` by name (`celery/beat.py:401-411`, verified) and no worker has a task under that name, so this sweep never runs | `bind=True, max_retries=3`, retry countdown 60 s | Would refresh OAuth2 credentials near expiry through `CredentialService.refresh_expiring_credentials` (PostgreSQL + Redis) |
