| `src.components.rag.tasks.cleanup_stale_awaiting_uploads` | `src/components/rag/tasks.py:235` | `default_queue` (`celery_config.py:77`) | beat `cleanup-stale-awaiting-uploads`, crontab `*/15 * * * *` (`:68-78`) | bare `@celery.task`, no retries | Deletes registry rows still in `AWAITING_UPLOAD` older than the presigned-URL lifetime (PostgreSQL) |
| `sweep_idle_sessions` | `src/components/sessions/tasks.py:20` | `default_queue` | beat `sweep-idle-sessions`, crontab from `ENV.CHAT_SESSION_SWEEP_CRON`, default `*/15 * * * *` (`celery_config.py:42-59`) | `bind=True, max_retries=3`, retry countdown 60 s | Two `UPDATE`s on `AiChatSession`: `ACTIVE -> IDLE` after `CHAT_SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, `ACTIVE/IDLE -> CLOSED` after `CHAT_SESSION_EXPIRY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (`:47-73`); deletes Redis hot-tier and session-memory keys for closed sessions (`:76-78`) |

### 4.2.3 Worker pools

`docker-compose.yml` runs four Celery processes from the same image: `celery-worker` (`:25-28`, no `-Q`, so it consumes all four declared queues), `campaign-dispatch-worker` (`:43-50`, `-Q campaign_dispatch_queue`), `campaign-post-call-worker` (`:65-68`, `-Q campaign_post_call_queue`) and `celery-beat` (`:83-86`), each worker with `--concurrency=4`. `k8s/campaign-dispatch-worker.yaml` defines two `Deployment`s with the same `-Q` flags (`:13-57`, `:75-115`) and two KEDA `ScaledObject`s that scale on Redis list length: `campaign_dispatch_queue` from 2 to 100 replicas at `listLength` 100 (`:59-72`) and `campaign_post_call_queue` from 2 to 50 at `listLength` 200 (`:117-130`). Those manifests reference `image: makunai-service:latest` and `secretRef: makunai-service-env` (`:35,49,93,107`), neither of which exists in the oc-config repo (FACTS). There is no Kubernetes manifest for the general `celery-worker` pool or for `celery-beat` in either repo.

### 4.2.4 Running async code from a sync worker

Celery calls task functions synchronously. Every task here wraps an `async def` body and drives it with one of three patterns:

- **New event loop per task** (campaign and RAG tasks). `_run(coro)` in `src/components/campaign/tasks.py:71-79` does `asyncio.new_event_loop()`, `set_event_loop`, `run_until_complete(_async_wrapper(coro))`, then `loop.close()`. `_async_wrapper` (`:22-69`) first builds a fresh `redis.asyncio` client with `RedisCache.init_redis_pool` and overrides `celery.container.redis_pool` with `providers.Object(fresh_redis)` (`:33-37`). The comment at `:22-32` explains why: `redis_pool` is a `providers.Resource` whose async initialiser is only awaited by `src/main.py`'s `init_resources()`, so inside the worker every provider that depends on it would otherwise resolve to an un-awaited `Future`. After the coroutine finishes it disposes the SQLAlchemy engine (`:40-46`), closes the Redis client (`:47-55`) and resets the override (`:69`). `src/components/rag/tasks.py:226-229,282-285` use the same new-loop pattern.
- **`asyncio.run`** (sessions, integrations, orchestration tasks). `sweep_idle_sessions` calls `asyncio.run(_async_sweep_idle_sessions())` (`src/components/sessions/tasks.py:24`); the coroutine builds its own `DatabaseSessionManager` and Redis client (`:31-36`) instead of using the container, and closes them in `finally` (`:86-91`). Same in `src/components/integrations/tasks.py:27` and `src/components/orchestration/tasks.py:42`.
- **`asyncio.get_event_loop().run_until_complete`** (context tasks, `src/components/context/tasks.py:57-58,90-91`). This reuses whatever loop the worker thread already has.

## 4.3 Voice call lifecycle

Two separate entry paths exist. Both end in the same PostgreSQL call-session row and the same MongoDB transcript.

### 4.3.1 LiveKit room path (browser WebRTC and relay-less PSTN)

Actors: the API process (`src/components/voice/controllers.py`), the LiveKit server, and the voice worker process (`python -m src.components.voice.worker start`, `docker-compose.yml:141-147`) built on `livekit-agents` 1.8.2 (locked).

1. **`POST /ai/v1/voice/token`** (`controllers.py:68-158`, `voice_router` prefix `/ai/v1/voice` at `:47`) or **`POST /ai/v1/voice/sessions`** (`:160-248`, status 201). Both read `partner_id` with `get_partner_id(request)`, reject a request whose `X-Agent-Secret` identity is scoped to a different `agent_id` with 403 (`:104-110`, `:192-198`), build an `InitiateCallRequest`, and call `VoiceService.initiate_call` (`:125`, `:219`). `/token` answers with `token`, `livekit_url`, `room_name`, `session_id` (`:127-135`); `/sessions` answers with the full `InitiateCallResponse` dict (`:220-223`).
2. **`VoiceService.initiate_call`** (`src/components/voice/services.py:86-289`): loads and validates the agent (`AgentNotReadyError` if not published), pre-creates the call record row in PostgreSQL to get `call_record_id`, builds `RoomMetadata` JSON, then `await self.__transport.create_room(room_name, metadata)` (`:201`) and `self.__transport.generate_tokens(...)` (`:205-210`). The transport is `LiveKitTransport` (`src/components/voice/adapters/livekit/livekit_transport.py:18`): `create_room` (`:53`) uses `api.LiveKitAPI` (`:39`), `generate_tokens` (`:108`) mints two `api.AccessToken` JSON Web Tokens with `VideoGrants` (`:126-150`), one for the caller and one for the agent. The service then upserts an `AiChatSession` row (`:261-272`) and returns `room_url`, `caller_token`, `livekit_url` (`:281-289`).
3. **LiveKit server -> worker job.** The browser joins the room with the caller token. The LiveKit server dispatches a job to the worker, which was started with `cli.run_app(WorkerOptions(entrypoint_fnc=entrypoint, prewarm_fnc=prewarm_process, ...))` (`src/components/voice/worker.py:755-770`). `prewarm_process` (`:249`) preloads plugins and the container in each forked job process.
4. **`entrypoint(ctx)`** (`worker.py:716-751`): `await ctx.connect(auto_subscribe=AudioOnly)` first (`:720`), classifies the call as `pstn` or `web` with `_is_pstn_call` (`:694-713`, room name prefix `call-` or metadata `caller_phone`), awaits the container (`:726`), runs backend warm-ups (`:736`), then `session = VoiceSessionHandler(ctx=ctx, container=container)`; `await session.setup()`; `await session.run()` (`:740-745`).
5. **`VoiceSessionHandler`** (`src/components/voice/session/handler.py:52`) is composed of mixins: `SessionBillingMixin`, `SessionEventRouterMixin`, `SessionLifecycleMixin` (`handler.py:24-27`). `setup()` (`src/components/voice/session/lifecycle.py:39-227`) calls `_connect` (`:46`), `_parse_metadata` (`:61`, reads the room metadata JSON written in step 2), `_load_agent_config` (`:86`), `_build_session` (`:177`) and `register_event_hooks` (`:182`).
6. **Pipeline build.** `_build_session` (`lifecycle.py:478-513`) constructs `VoicePipelineBuilder` (`src/components/voice/pipeline/builder.py:39`) and calls `build_session` (`:96`), which selects cascaded, speech-to-speech or half-cascade (`:155`, `:291`, `:317`) from the agent's `voice_mode_config`. The cascaded path uses `build_vad` (`:344`, `SileroVADAdapter` in `adapters/vad/silero_vad.py:13`), `build_stt` (`:367`, for example `DeepgramSTTAdapter` in `adapters/stt/deepgram_stt.py:20`), `build_llm` (`:396`, returns `LLMCoreAdapter`, `adapters/llm_adapter.py:49`, which wraps the same `LLMEngine` used for text chat through `LLMTurnCore` in `adapters/llm_core_bridge.py:48`), and `build_tts` (`:415`, for example `CartesiaTTSAdapter` in `adapters/tts/cartesia_tts.py:18`). The realtime path uses `_build_realtime_plugin` (`:198`, for example `GeminiRealtimeAdapter` in `adapters/realtime/gemini_realtime.py:132`).
7. **`run()`** (`lifecycle.py:229-300`): starts background tasks `_mark_in_progress` and `_upsert_chat_session` (`:234-242`), registers a `disconnected` room handler (`:256-258`), and calls `await self.session.start(agent=MakunVoiceAgent(...), room=self.ctx.room, ...)` (`:260-267`; `MakunVoiceAgent` is `src/components/voice/session/agent.py:223`). The call then runs inside `livekit.agents.AgentSession`.
8. **History and transcripts.** `register_event_hooks` (`src/components/voice/session/event_router.py:25`) attaches handlers such as `on_transcribed` (`:186`). Each finished turn goes through `sync_conversation_turn` (`src/components/voice/session/history_sync.py:91`) -> `_persist_turn` (`:30`) -> `history_manager.append_messages(...)` (`:77`), the same MongoDB transcript writer the text chat uses. The transcript engine (`handler.py:28-47` imports) runs slot-capture and normalisation on top.
9. **End.** When the room disconnects, `run()` returns and calls `_finalize(SessionStatus.COMPLETED)` (`:300`). `_finalize` (`lifecycle.py:1490-1535`) computes the duration, calls `repo.finalize_voice_session(...)` on `call_session_repository` (`:1506-1512`), drains queued transcript writes with `drain_session_tasks` (`history_sync.py:188`), and exports the transcript to the campaign tables (`:1527`). The browser can also call **`POST /ai/v1/voice/sessions/{session_id}/end`** (`controllers.py:250-322`) -> `VoiceService.end_session` (`services.py:291-396`), which calls `finalize_voice_session` (`:341`), `transport.delete_room` (`:363`) and `session_service.mark_idle` (`:378`). The controller docstring says the worker also calls this route on disconnect (`:264-265`); the worker actually writes the row directly through the repository (step 9), not through HTTP.

### 4.3.2 PSTN SIP relay path (WebSocket, no LiveKit room)

This path is for calls that holler-service bridges directly from the Tata Tele SIP trunk (`src/components/voice/session/ws_relay_session.py:1-16`, `docs/architecture/07_PSTN_SIP_RELAY.md`).

1. **`POST /ai/v1/voice/ws-ticket`** (`controllers.py:373-399`). Normal `AuthMiddleware` applies (holler-service uses an `API-KEY`, per the docstring `:381-386`). The handler calls `get_partner_id(request)` only to enforce auth (`:393`) and returns `mint_stream_token(payload.agent_id, ENV.MAKUNAI_STREAM_SECRET)` (`:395`). The token format is `base64url(agent_id|expires_at|nonce).hex(hmac_sha256(payload))` with a 300-second lifetime (`src/components/voice/platform/stream_token.py:15,28,31-42`). `MAKUNAI_STREAM_SECRET` has a hardcoded non-empty default in `src/core/environment.py:121-123` (redacted). The module docstring calls the ticket "single-use", but `verify_stream_token` (`:45-66`) checks only signature and expiry and keeps no nonce store, so a ticket is reusable until it expires.
2. **WebSocket `/ai/v1/voice/stream/{agent_id}`** (`controllers.py:403-462`). Because the scope is `websocket`, none of the `BaseHTTPMiddleware` layers and not `CORSMiddleware` run (see 4.1.1); only `ContextMiddleware` sees the connection. Authentication is therefore solely: query params `partner_id` (digits) and `token` must be present (`:424-428`, close code 4401), `verify_stream_token(token, ENV.MAKUNAI_STREAM_SECRET)` must return an agent id (`:431-434`, 4401), and it must equal the path `agent_id` (`:435-442`, 4403). `partner_id` is taken from the query string as is; it is not covered by the HMAC (`:451`). Then `await websocket.accept()` and `WsRelaySession(...).run()` (`:445-453`).
3. **`WsRelaySession.run`** (`ws_relay_session.py:258-346`). It waits up to 10 s for the first text frame, which must be a Twilio-shaped `{"event": "start", "start": {...}}` message (`_wait_for_start`, `:350-396`, `receive_text` at `:353`, check at `:362-363`); the `start` block carries `callSid`/`streamSid`, `session_id`, `context_data`, `caller_did` and `caller_phone` from holler-service (`:366-386`). In parallel it loads `RoomMetadata` and `AgentConfig` (`_load_session`, `:456`), upserts the relay session row (`:314`), then branches on `PipelineConfigResolver.resolve_voice_mode(agent_config)` (`:326`): `CASCADED` -> `_run_cascaded` (`:329`, body `:1480`), `SPEECH_TO_SPEECH` -> `_run_speech_to_speech` (`:331`, body `:1797`); any other mode closes the socket with 1011 (`:333-345`).
4. **Gemini realtime.** `_run_speech_to_speech` resolves provider, model, voice and language with `PipelineConfigResolver` (`:1800-1803`) and drives the realtime plugin directly without an `AgentSession` (module docstring `:13-16`). Audio in: `_receive_loop` (`:1355-1390`) parses each JSON frame, and for `event == "media"` base64-decodes `media.payload` and converts mu-law to 16-bit PCM with `audioop.ulaw2lin` (`:1369-1374`) before pushing frames to the model. Audio out: `_sender_loop` (`:1455-1470`) base64-encodes mu-law chunks into `{"event": "media", "media": {"payload": ...}}` frames and sends `{"event": "clear"}` on barge-in (`:1459-1467`), through `_send` -> `ws.send_text` (`:1472-1474`). A `stop` event ends the receive loop (`:1383`).
5. Turn history is persisted with the same `history_sync` helpers and the session row is finalised by the relay session itself; `WebSocketDisconnect` is logged and any other exception closes with 1011 (`controllers.py:455-462`).

## 4.4 Inbound webhooks

Exactly three inbound webhook routes exist in `src/` (grep verified). All are in `src/components/campaign/controllers/webhooks.py` on `campaign_webhook_router` with prefix `/ai/v1/voice/webhooks` (`:23-25`), included by `src/routes/base.py:63`.

| Route | Caller | Auth | What it triggers |
|---|---|---|---|
| `POST /ai/v1/voice/webhooks/babbler-reply` (`:28-139`) | babbler-service, when a WhatsApp or RCS reply arrives from a campaign recipient (`:43-46`) | On `AuthMiddleware` `excluded_paths` (`authentication.py:48`). Handler compares header `X-Webhook-Secret` with `ENV.BABBLER_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (`:48-49`); the env var has an empty default (`src/core/environment.py:29`), so when unset every call is rejected with 401 | Looks up Redis key `babbler:awaiting:{receiver}:{sender}` (`:63-67`, prefix from `src/core/redis.py:15`); if no fallback is awaiting, returns 200 "Not a tracked fallback reply" (`:69-73`). Otherwise dedups on `babbler:msg:{message_id}` (`:83-86`), runs `TimeExtractionService.extract_callback_time` on the text (`:95-97`), and calls `recipient_repo.apply_disposition_outcome` with status `CALLBACK_SCHEDULED` or `REPLIED_NO_CALLBACK` (`:113-124`) in PostgreSQL |
| `POST /ai/v1/voice/webhooks/holler-cdr` (`:146-167`) | holler-service, legacy curated Call Detail Record (CDR) payload (`:156`) | Not excluded, so the normal `AuthMiddleware` applies and the handler reads `get_partner_id(request)` (`:158`) | `CampaignService.handle_holler_cdr_webhook(payload, partner_id)` (`src/components/campaign/services/campaign.py:1108`) |
| `POST /ai/v1/voice/webhooks/tata-dialer-cdr` (`:170-224`) | holler-service relaying Tata Tele's raw dialer webhook verbatim, with `partner_id` injected into the body (`:181-186`) | On `excluded_paths` (`authentication.py:45`). Handler compares `X-Webhook-Secret` with `ENV.CDR_WEBHOOK_RELAY_SECRET` (`:193-200`); that variable has a hardcoded non-empty default in `src/core/environment.py:37-40` (redacted), so the check passes with the in-code value when the env var is absent | Pops `partner_id` from the body (`:209`; missing -> 200 with `missing_partner_id`) and calls `CampaignService.handle_tata_dialer_webhook(int(partner_id), raw_payload)` (`campaign.py:1162`), which maps CDR fields and applies the disposition to the recipient |

**No WhatsApp/Meta inbound webhook exists in this checkout.** `docs/architecture/01_SYSTEM_OVERVIEW.md:19` lists "WhatsApp Inbound Webhook" as done with "Meta Cloud API webhook verify + signed inbound message routing", and `:149` labels `integrations/` as holding a WhatsApp webhook. There is no route for it, no `hub.challenge` verification and no `X-Hub-Signature` check anywhere in `src/` (grep verified). What exists is: (a) the `babbler-reply` route above, which handles only campaign fallback replies and never enters the chat pipeline; and (b) WhatsApp conversations that arrive as ordinary `POST /ai/v1/chat/message` calls with `interface == Interface.WHATSAPP` (`src/components/common/channel.py:16`), made by babbler-service, which "concatenates SSE verbatim" (`src/components/chat/services.py:684-689`); the chat service converts Markdown to WhatsApp dialect for those turns and hashes the WhatsApp phone for per-owner limits (`src/components/chat/utils.py:105-110`). The only Meta Graph API use is outbound: `WA_GRAPH_API_BASE` (`src/components/chat/media/constants.py:73`) is used to fetch WhatsApp media by id in `src/components/chat/media/services.py:549`.

## 4.5 Outbound calls to other company services

| Target | Protocol | Code | Auth it sends |
|---|---|---|---|
| console-service | gRPC (grpcio 1.80.0 locked) | `src/grpc_client/**`, `src/grpc_interceptor/auth_interceptor.py` | metadata header `x-api-key` with a hardcoded literal string (`grpc_client.py:52,65`, value redacted) |
| holler-service | HTTP via `HttpClient` (`src/components/common/http_client.py`) | `src/components/campaign/services/did_selection.py`, `dispatch.py`, `src/components/agents/controllers.py:230-233` | partner's console API key or the caller's Bearer token; `list-ai-agent-dids` sends none |
| babbler-service | HTTP | `src/components/campaign/services/babbler_client.py`, `src/components/tools/clients/service_clients.py:59` | caller's Bearer token for reads; `ENV.BABBLER_SERVICE_API_KEY` for `POST /template/send` |
| maglo | HTTP (httpx 0.28.1 locked) | `src/components/tools/clients/service_clients.py:58`, `src/components/tools/executor.py:1095,1199` | caller's own auth header replayed |
| MailMG | HTTP (requests 2.33.1 locked) | `src/components/email_service/services.py` | header `X-Channel-Key` = `ENV.MAILMG_CHANNEL_KEY` |

**console-service gRPC.** `GRPCClient` (`src/grpc_client/grpc_client.py:19`) targets `CONSOLE_GRPC_HOST:CONSOLE_GRPC_PORT` (`:24-29`). `create_channel` (`:33-40`) picks TLS when `ENV.CA != "None"` (`:35`; the default of `CA` in `src/core/environment.py:47` is the literal string `None`, so a plain-text channel is the default): `_create_secure_channel` (`:42-61`) base64-decodes `CA`, builds `grpc.ssl_channel_credentials(root_certificates=...)` and sets `grpc.ssl_target_name_override` to `console-service.makunaiglobal.ai` (`:53`); `_create_insecure_channel` (`:63-67`) uses `grpc.aio.insecure_channel`. Both attach `ApiKeyClientInterceptor` (`src/grpc_interceptor/auth_interceptor.py:7-26`), which appends `("x-api-key", <literal>)` to every call's metadata (`:12-13`). `call_with_retry` (`:76-96`) retries `AioRpcError` three times with a 2-second sleep and returns `None` after the last failure, so callers see `None` rather than an exception. `RPCServiceFactory.get_service` (`src/grpc_client/rpc_service_factory.py:16-25`) returns a **new** `AuthServiceClient` or `ApiKeyServiceClient`, each constructing a new channel (`grpc_client.py:31`), per call; `AuthMiddleware` calls it on every Bearer and API-KEY request (`authentication.py:106,157`). The stubs used are `AuthServiceStub.ValidateToken` (`src/grpc_client/client_services/auth_service_client.py:21-46`, returns `is_active`, `user_session_id`, `user_id`, `partner_id`), `GetUserChildHierarchy` (`:48`), `get_user_details` (`:109`, used by the error e-mail) and `ApiKeyServiceStub.ValidateApiKey` (`src/grpc_client/client_services/api_key_service_client.py:18-38`, returns `id`, `key`, `partner_id`, `is_active`). `validate_token` logs the raw bearer token at `auth_service_client.py:22-26`. The campaign dispatcher also calls `get_api_key_by_partner_id` (`api_key_service_client.py:39`) to fetch the partner's key for holler (`src/components/campaign/services/dispatch.py:916-917`, cached in Redis `campaign:partner_holler_api_key:{partner_id}` for 15 min, `:925-927`, per survey `campaign-cdr.json`).

**holler-service HTTP.** `DidSelectionService` (`src/components/campaign/services/did_selection.py`) calls `GET {HOLLER_SERVICE_BASE_URL}/dids/ai-agent/available` (`:81-82`), `POST /dids/campaign-claim` (`:122-123`), `POST /dids/campaign-release` (`:163-164`) and `GET /dids/list-ai-agent-dids` (`:202-203`). `DispatchService` places each outbound AI call with `POST {HOLLER_SERVICE_BASE_URL}/call` carrying header `API-KEY` = the partner's console key (`dispatch.py:644-645`, retry loop `:1084-1092` up to `HOLLER_CALL_MAX_ATTEMPTS`). `GET /ai/v1/agents` also lists AI DIDs from holler on every call (`src/components/agents/controllers.py:230-233`, failures swallowed). In the other direction holler-service calls `ws-ticket`, the `stream` WebSocket (4.3.2) and the `tata-dialer-cdr` webhook (4.4).

**babbler-service HTTP.** `BabblerClient` (`src/components/campaign/services/babbler_client.py:18`) reads `GET /partner/config` (`:110`), `GET /contacts` (`:143,208`), `GET /audiences` (`:181`) and `GET /template` by forwarding the live user's auth header from `get_request_auth()` (`:9,64`); `POST /template/send` (`:274`) uses the static `ENV.BABBLER_SERVICE_API_KEY` (`:38`) because fallback sends run in Celery with no user context (`src/core/environment.py:26-28` comment). The tool executor has a second pooled client for `TargetService.BABBLER` (`src/components/tools/clients/service_clients.py:59`).

**maglo.** `ToolServiceClients` builds one `httpx.AsyncClient` per target (`src/components/tools/clients/service_clients.py:58-60,72`) with base URLs `ENV.MAGLO_API_BASE_URL`, `ENV.BABBLER_API_BASE_URL`, `ENV.INTERNAL_API_BASE_URL`. `ToolExecutor` fetches the client for `tool.target_service` and sends the request (`src/components/tools/executor.py:1095,1199`). The auth header is the caller's own header replayed from the request `ContextVar` by `src/components/tools/auth_resolver.py:95-97`; in the voice worker no such context exists and empty headers are sent (`:98-100`). `INTERNAL` tools are executed in-process instead, so that client is unused (survey `tools-integrations.json`, not re-verified).

**MailMG e-mail.** `EmailService` (`src/components/email_service/services.py:18`) posts JSON to `ENV.MAILMG_API_URL` with header `X-Channel-Key` = `ENV.MAILMG_CHANNEL_KEY` and sender `ENV.MAILMG_CLIENT_NAME` (`:31-33`, `:159-170`), using blocking `requests.post` with a 10-second timeout and three attempts (`:166-180`). `send_email` (`:77`) is synchronous; `ErrorHandlingMiddleware` calls it from the async event loop (`exception.py:197`), which blocks the loop for up to the request timeout on each failure.

## 4.6 Streaming responses

`POST /ai/v1/chat/message` (`src/components/chat/controllers.py:48-195`, `chat_router` prefix `/ai/v1/chat` at `:41`) is the one Server-Sent Events (SSE) endpoint for text chat; `POST .../tool-calls/{id}/consent` (`:197-237`) reuses the same mechanism.

**How the `StreamingResponse` is built.** The controller first runs the pre-stream gate `ChatService.check_text_rate_limits` (`:128-133`), which returns ordinary JSON errors: 400 `BadRequestResponse` for `SessionClosedError` (`:136`), 429 `TooManyRequestsResponse` for `RateLimitExceededError` (`:143-148`). Then it creates the async generator `chat_service.stream_chat_message(payload, ...)` (`:150-156`) and returns `ChatStreamResponse(format_sse_event(stream_generator, logger))` (`:157`). `ChatStreamResponse` (`src/components/common/responses.py:425-461`) is a `StreamingResponse` with status 200, `media_type="text/event-stream"`, and headers `Cache-Control: no-cache` and `X-Accel-Buffering: no` (`:449-452`). `format_sse_event` (`:464-520`) writes one `data: <json>\n\n` frame per event, using `model_dump_json(exclude_none=True)` for Pydantic models (`:494`); an event whose `type` is `heartbeat` becomes the SSE comment `: ping\n\n` (`:489-490`), which keeps proxies from closing the connection without reaching the client transcript. HTTP 200 and the headers are sent before any model work starts, so every error after that point arrives as an event, not as a status code. `RequestResponseLoggingMiddleware` detects the `StreamingResponse`/`text/event-stream` and does not buffer it (`logging_request_and_response.py:82-98`).

**Event types.** The real names are the `StreamEventType` enum in `src/components/chat/constants.py:22-31`: `delta`, `tool_call`, `transfer`, `handoff`, `done`, `error`, `rag_context`, `tool_result`, `tool_consent_required`. Each is a Pydantic model in `src/components/chat/schemas.py` whose `type` field is a `Literal` of one enum value: `TextChunkStreamEvent` (`:99`, `content`), `ToolCallStreamEvent` (`:106`), `TransferStreamEvent` (`:115`), `HandoffStreamEvent` (`:130`), `DoneStreamEvent` (`:146`), `ErrorStreamEvent` (`:154`, `message`), `RagContextStreamEvent` (`:161`), `ToolResultStreamEvent` (`:168`), `ToolConsentRequiredStreamEvent` (`:177`). `schemas.py:65-69` also defines a tenth class, `CompletionStreamEvent` (`type: "complete"`), and puts it in the `ChatStreamEvent` union at `:196`; `complete` is not a `StreamEventType` member and nothing in `src/` yields it (grep verified), so it never appears on the wire. `ChatService._map_llm_event` (`src/components/chat/services.py:400-528`) converts each `LLMStreamEvent` from the engine into one of these (`:426`, `:438`, `:447`, `:460`, `:474`, `:486`, `:498`, `:513`). The route's OpenAPI description (`controllers.py:54-60`) lists only four of the nine types.

**What ends the stream.** Normal completion: the engine's end-of-turn event is mapped to `DoneStreamEvent` (`services.py:486`), the generator returns, and `format_sse_event` writes the final frame `data: [DONE]\n\n` (`responses.py:504`). Consent pause: a `ToolConsentRequiredStreamEvent` is yielded and the loop continues (`:710-712`, `continue`); the generator ends when `LLMEngine.run_turn` (`:691`) stops yielding, then `_finalize_turn_counters` runs with `consent_paused=True` (`:721-727`) and `[DONE]` follows. Failure inside the service: `SessionClosedError` or any of `ChatServiceError`, `LLMException`, `SQLAlchemyError`, `RedisError`, `Exception` is turned into `ErrorStreamEvent(message=str(exc))` (`:733`, `:739`, `:754`) and the session is marked closed with reason `error` (`:741`, `:756`); then `[DONE]`. Failure inside the formatter itself: `format_sse_event` writes `{"type": "error", "message": "Stream error"}` then `[DONE]` and re-raises (`:516-519`). Client disconnect surfaces as `asyncio.CancelledError`, which is logged and re-raised (`:508-511`). In every case `stream_chat_message`'s `finally` calls `_release_turn_resources` (`:760`), which deletes the per-session Redis lock and stores the collected events under the idempotency key for replay (`:559`).

**Text-chat turn, in call order (function names grep-verified):**

```
client
  -> POST /ai/v1/chat/message (JSON body: session_id, agent_id, message, interface ...)
  -> ContextMiddleware.set_context                         src/middlewares/context.py:37
  -> CORSMiddleware
  -> AllowedHostsMiddleware.dispatch (JSON pre-read)       src/middlewares/allowed_host.py:23
  -> AuthMiddleware.dispatch -> request.state.user          src/middlewares/authentication.py:26
  -> ErrorHandlingMiddleware.dispatch                      src/middlewares/exception.py:209
  -> RequestResponseLoggingMiddleware.dispatch             src/middlewares/logging_request_and_response.py:17
  -> ChatController.process_chat_message                   src/components/chat/controllers.py:88
       get_partner_id(request)                             src/components/common/request_identity.py:4
       ChatService.check_text_rate_limits                  src/components/chat/services.py:764
       return ChatStreamResponse(format_sse_event(...))    controllers.py:157  (HTTP 200 + headers sent)
  -> ChatService.stream_chat_message                       services.py:586
       _claim_idempotency_lock / _acquire_session_lock     services.py:615,617   (Redis)
       _enforce_text_limits                                services.py:632
       SessionService.upsert_active                        services.py:642       (PostgreSQL)
       AgentService.load_agent_config                      services.py:653 -> agents/services.py:163
       AgentService.build_llm_request                      agents/services.py:275
  -> LLMEngine.run_turn(llm_request, bypass_db=False)      services.py:691 -> src/components/llm/engine.py:109
  -> LLMCore.generate_response_stream                      src/components/llm/core.py:162
       ContextOrchestrator.build_context                   core.py:239 -> src/components/context/context_orchestrator.py:64
         (Redis session memory + hot tier, Mongo sliding window / summary blocks, RAG)
       MongoHistoryManager.append_messages (user turn)     core.py:348 -> src/components/history_manager/manager.py:102
       LLMProviderFactory.get_provider                     core.py:373 -> src/components/llm/factory.py:77
       _handle_tool_calls_loop -> provider.stream(...)     core.py:518,549 -> llm/providers/{openai,anthropic,gemini,groq}_llm.py
       -> TextChunk events ... or ToolCall events
  -> LLMEngine.run_turn: ToolExecutor.execute_tool          engine.py (tool batch) -> src/components/tools/executor.py:280
       (maglo/babbler/integration HTTP, sub-agents, RAG search); results appended to history, loop continues
       MongoHistoryManager.append_messages (assistant)     core.py:482
  -> ChatService._map_llm_event -> yield ChatStreamEvent   services.py:400
  -> format_sse_event: "data: {...}\n\n" per event         src/components/common/responses.py:464
  -> ChatService._finalize_turn_counters                   services.py:721
  -> "data: [DONE]\n\n"; finally _release_turn_resources   responses.py:504; services.py:760
  -> client
```

# 5. External services inventory

Every network peer, model download and managed store this service talks to. One row per service. "kind" says how the code reaches it. "env vars" lists variable NAMES only (values are never shown; several have hardcoded non-empty defaults in `src/core/environment.py`, marked "redacted default"). "code location" is the line where the client is built or the call is made. Rows were built from the survey file `external_services.json` and then re-checked with grep against `src/` on 2026-10-10.

Notes on the table:

- Rows marked "not verified" could not be tied to an SDK import, URL or env-var reader inside `src/`. The DigitalOcean row is the important one: the whole object-storage layer is inside the missing submodule, so bucket layout, region and client options cannot be documented from this checkout.
- Most one-shot outbound HTTP calls go through `src/components/common/http_client.py` (shared `httpx.AsyncClient` per event loop, `:122-143`). Exceptions: MailMG (`requests`, synchronous, inside async middleware); the RAG worker file download (`requests.get`, `src/components/rag/tasks.py:70`); the Sarvam TTS adapter (own `httpx.AsyncClient`, `src/components/voice/adapters/tts/sarvam_tts.py:67,88-90`); the MCP client (`src/components/tools/clients/mcp_client.py:66,206-211`); the pooled per-target tool clients used by `ToolExecutor` (`src/components/tools/clients/service_clients.py:72,82-87`; the comment at `executor.py:140-141` says they are not a candidate for `HttpClient`); `OAuth2Client` (`src/components/integrations/core/oauth2_client.py:33,56,85`); the DCR probe at `src/components/integrations/mcp/dcr_service.py:95`; and the simulation-image download at `src/components/skin_analysis/engines/gpt_image_simulation_engine.py:92`.
- The voice worker opens raw TCP/TLS warm-up connections at start to `api.groq.com`, `api.openai.com`, `api.cartesia.ai` and `api.deepgram.com` (`src/components/voice/worker.py:439-466`).

# 6. Data stores

## 6.1 PostgreSQL tables

Table-name constants live in `src/database/constants.py` (59 lines). `src/components/campaign/constants.py` was read fully: it holds enums, Redis key templates (6.3) and Celery queue names, but NO table-name constants; every campaign table name comes from `src/database/constants.py:37-59`. The model column says which SQLAlchemy class sets `__tablename__` to the constant. "Usage" was checked by grepping each constant name across `src/` and `alembic/` excluding the constants file. All models inherit the shared `MetaData` columns from `src/database/base.py` except `AiChatSession`, which inherits `Base` directly (`src/components/sessions/models.py:23`).

Summary of duplicates and dead constants: 11 of the 52 table constants (lines 1-31, 35, 38-44 and 47-59; `:33-34` are the two Mongo collection names) have no model and are referenced nowhere outside the constants file (`DISPOSITION_CODE_TABLE`, `DISPOSITION_RULE_TABLE`, `AGENT_HANDOFF_LOG_TABLE`, `AGENT_HANDOFF_FINDINGS_TABLE`, and all seven "Dev 2" campaign constants at lines 38-44). Five pairs point at the same physical table under two names: `makun_ai_disposition_codes` (:7/:52), `makun_ai_disposition_rules` (:8/:53), `makun_ai_voice_dnc` (:40/:54), `makun_ai_fallback_flows` (:43/:55) and `makun_ai_fallback_flow_nodes` (:44/:56). Tables not named by any constant but present in the schema: `digital_assets` (FK target from the media-constant models, owned by the missing submodule) and `alembic_version`.

## 6.2 MongoDB collections

Exactly two collections are referenced in `src/`, both through constants; no collection name is used as a bare string literal (grep for `.collection(`, `get_collection(` and `db["..."]` verified). Both live in database `ENV.MONGO_DB` (default `makunai-service`, `src/core/environment.py:58`) and are accessed through `DocDatabaseSessionManager.collection()` (`src/core/doc_db.py:9`).

| collection | constant (`src/database/constants.py:line`) | repository class, file:line | document purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| `makun_ai_chat_transcripts` | `MONGO_COLLECTION_CHAT_TRANSCRIPTS` (:33) | `ChatTranscriptRepository`, `src/components/chat/repositories.py:13` (uses at :46-47, :70, :98, :135, :163, :224, :286, :305, :330, :379); also `CompressionRepository`, `src/components/context/repository.py:20` (:70-71, :140-141) | One document per (`session_id`, `partner_id`): `agent_id`, `messages[]` (role, content, medium, tool fields; capped by `$slice` at `ENV.MAX_SESSION_MESSAGES`), `session_memory{}` map of facts, timestamps. Written by chat, voice (`src/components/voice/session/history_sync.py:77-83`) and the Celery tasks `compress_session` / `persist_session_fact` |
| `makun_ai_session_summary_blocks` | `MONGO_COLLECTION_SESSION_SUMMARY_BLOCKS` (:34) | `CompressionRepository`, `src/components/context/repository.py:20` (:163-164 insert, :190-191 read) | Compressed summaries of older turn ranges (`start_turn_index`, `end_turn_index`, `summary_text`, key decisions), injected as system messages for text sessions |

Index creation code exists at `src/components/chat/repositories.py:305-318` but, per the chat survey, is never called at startup.

## 6.3 Redis key families

All keys share one logical database (`CACHE_DB`). The namespace prefixes `console:`, `user_service:`, `maglo:`, `holler:`, `babbler:`, `makun_ai:` are declared in `RedisCache.KeysPrefix` (`src/core/redis.py:10-16`); only `babbler:` and `makun_ai:` are written by this repo. Patterns below were merged from the survey and from `grep -rhoE '"[a-z_]+:\{' src` (first hit per prefix at `src/middlewares/authentication.py:105,150`, `src/components/campaign/constants.py:120`, `src/components/chat/media/services.py:39`, `src/components/rag/constants.py:81-83`, `src/components/voice/services.py:511`, `src/components/voice/session/handler.py:170`, `src/components/context/hot_tier.py:26`, `src/components/chat/media/constants.py:63`, `src/components/voice/session/response_cache.py:50`, `src/components/voice/constants/session.py:18-19`, `src/components/context/session_memory.py:33`, `src/components/tools/credentials/constants.py:9`). Four grep hits are NOT Redis keys: `chain:{...}` (`ws_relay_session.py:1334`, TTS chain label), `nudge:{}` (`:1024`, label), `gemini:{}:{}` (`realtime_error_alerts.py:110`, in-memory cooldown dict) and `data:{mime}` (`content_blocks.py:300`, data URI).

| key pattern | purpose | TTL | defined / written at |
|---|---|---|---|
| `token:{bearer_token}` | Cache of console `ValidateToken` result (JSON with partner and user ids) | 300 s (`setex`) | `src/middlewares/authentication.py:105` (get), `:130` (set) |
| `api_key:{api_key}` | Intended cache of `ValidateApiKey`; read only, never written in this repo | none | `src/middlewares/authentication.py:150` |
| `makun_ai:agent_config:{partner_id}:{agent_id}` | JSON `AgentConfig` cache-aside; deleted on every agent mutation (plus a delayed second delete) | 300 s (`AGENT_CONFIG_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`, `src/components/agents/constants.py:158`) | `src/components/agents/services.py:109-110` (key), `:261` (setex) |
| `makun_ai:agent_secret:{op}:{agent_id}` | Fixed-window counter for secret create/regenerate and session mint (`INCR` + `EXPIRE`) | window from `SECRET_RATE_LIMITS` (`src/components/agents/secret_rate_limit.py:7`) | `src/components/agents/secret_rate_limit.py:25-29` |
| `makun_ai:session_token:{token}` | Minted session token payload; written by `POST /agents/session`, read nowhere (survey) | 3600 s (`SESSION_TOKEN_TTL_SECONDS`, `src/components/agents/session_token_utils.py:4`) | `src/components/agents/services.py:834-835` |
| `makun_ai:config:chat_rate_per_min:{partner_id}` | Partner chat rate limit mirrored from Postgres on every config read/write | none | `src/components/partner_config/services.py:216-219` |
| `makun_ai:config:use_custom_api_key:{partner_id}` | `"true"`/`"false"` flag telling `LLMProviderFactory` to use the partner's own key | none | `src/components/partner_config/services.py:243`; template `src/components/llm/constants.py:282`; also written by the factory fallback `src/components/llm/factory.py:195` |
| `makun_ai:config:meta_config:{partner_id}` | Full partner `meta_config` JSON (provider API keys in plaintext, rate-limit and orchestration flags) read by chat and LLM factory | none | `src/components/partner_config/constants.py:32-41` (key builder); factory fallback write `src/components/llm/factory.py:197-199` |
| `makun_ai:llm_provider:{provider_type}` | Presence flag that a global-key provider instance was built | 3600 s (`LLM_PROVIDER_CACHE_TTL_SECS`, `src/components/llm/constants.py:216`) | `src/components/llm/factory.py:144-147` (set), `:335-349` (key) |
| `makun_ai:oauth_refresh_lock:{credential_id}` | `SET NX` single-flight lock for OAuth refresh across workers; released by Lua compare-and-delete | 30 s (`OAUTH_REFRESH_LOCK_TTL_SECONDS`) | `src/components/integrations/constants.py:167-168`; `src/components/integrations/core/vault.py:179-210` |
| `oauth_state:{state_token}` | OAuth authorization state (partner, user, provider, redirect, PKCE verifier, session/agent ids) | 600 s (`OAUTH_STATE_TTL_SECS`) | `src/components/integrations/constants.py:8-9`; written `src/components/integrations/services/credentials.py:169-173` |
| `tool_cred:{credential_id}` | Resolved auth headers/params for a partner tool credential; deleted on update and on HTTP 401 | 300 s (`TOOL_CREDENTIAL_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`) | `src/components/tools/credentials/constants.py:9-12`; `src/components/tools/auth_resolver.py:159-184` |
| `idempotency:{partner_id}:{key}` and `...:lock` | Replay cache of a turn's SSE (Server-Sent Events) list, and an in-flight lock against duplicate concurrent turns | cache 300 s (`getattr(ENV, "IDEMPOTENCY_TTL_SECONDS", 300)`, field absent so always 300); lock 60 s | `src/components/chat/constants.py:37-38`; `src/components/chat/services.py:362` (lock), `:580-582` (cache) |
| `orch:lock:{session_id}` | Per-session turn lock (not partner-scoped) | 120 s (`SESSION_LOCK_TTL_SECONDS`) | `src/components/chat/constants.py:48-49`; `src/components/chat/services.py:383` |
| `hot_tier:{session_id}` (list) | Last `HOT_TIER_MAX_TURNS` turns as JSON; preferred over the Mongo sliding window | 3600 s (`2 * SWEEPER_ACTIVE_TO_IDLE_SECONDS`, `src/components/sessions/constants.py:19`), refreshed on each access. `ENV.HOT_TIER_TTL_SECONDS` (`environment.py:177`, default 1800) and `ENV.SESSION_MEMORY_TTL_SECONDS` (`:180`, default 86400) exist but are not read by the code (grep verified; only a test references the first) | `src/components/context/hot_tier.py:22,26,43` |

| `campaign:did_inflight:{did_number}` | Per-DID in-flight reservation counter, Lua INCR-under-cap | 30 min (`DID_INFLIGHT_TTL_SECONDS`, `:162`) | `src/components/campaign/constants.py:114`; `src/components/campaign/services/did_selection.py:18-30` |

delivery path.

```

Legend (10 lines):

1. `HTTP/SSE` = HTTPS request/response; chat and prompt-builder routes stream Server-Sent Events (SSE) back to the caller.
2. `gRPC` = the console-service calls over grpcio with an `x-api-key` metadata header added by `src/grpc_interceptor` (`src/grpc_client/client_services/`, protos in `src/protos/`). TLS only when `CA` is set; the default is a plaintext channel (`src/grpc_client/grpc_client.py:35,63-67`; `CA` defaults to the string `None`, `src/core/environment.py:47`).
3. `WS` = WebSocket. The only WebSocket route is `/ai/v1/voice/stream/{agent_id}` (`src/components/voice/controllers.py:403`); it checks the HMAC ticket from `src/components/voice/platform/stream_token.py` and nothing else, because middlewares 3 to 6 subclass `BaseHTTPMiddleware` and skip non-HTTP scopes (verified in starlette 0.46.2 `middleware/base.py:98-101`), and middleware 2 (`CORSMiddleware`, pure ASGI) skips them with its own check (`middleware/cors.py:76`).
4. `Redis lists` = Celery's Redis broker; a queue is a Redis list, so this is "AMQP-style" messaging without a real AMQP broker (`src/makunai_celery/celery_app.py:22-30`; KEDA reads the same lists: `k8s/campaign-dispatch-worker.yaml:67-71,125-129`).
5. `WebRTC` = browser audio through the LiveKit server; the voice worker joins the room with `livekit-agents` (`src/components/voice/worker.py`). `SIP` audio never reaches LiveKit on the relay path.
6. Header names in the client boxes come from `src/middlewares/authentication.py:57` (API-KEY), `:69` (X-Agent-Secret) and `:81-90` (Bearer); Bearer tokens are cached in Redis for 300 s after one `ValidateToken` RPC (`:106-129`).
7. babbler-service is both a client (it calls `POST /ai/v1/chat/message` with `interface=whatsapp`; `src/components/chat/services.py:684-688` converts output to WhatsApp dialect because "Babbler concatenates SSE verbatim") and a webhook source (`POST /ai/v1/voice/webhooks/babbler-reply`, `src/components/campaign/controllers/webhooks.py:28`, shared-secret header, used only for campaign fallback replies).
8. holler-service is called by the Celery dispatch pool (`src/components/campaign/services/dispatch.py:644,1086`; `ENV.HOLLER_SERVICE_BASE_URL` in `src/core/environment.py:15`) and calls back with CDR webhooks (`webhooks.py:146,170`) and the relay WebSocket.
9. Vendor mapping: LLM text providers in `src/components/llm/providers/`; voice vendors in `src/components/voice/pipeline/registry.py:38-84`; worker plugin preloads in `src/components/voice/worker.py:63-71,94`; MCP client in `src/components/tools/clients/mcp_client.py:83`; MailMG POST in `src/components/email_service/services.py:168`; maglo-service only as a tool target (`src/components/tools/clients/service_clients.py:58`).
10. Delivery: `.github/workflows/deployment.yaml:49` tags the image `makun-ai:integrationlatest`; the ArgoCD Application (`oc-config/argocd/apps/makun-ai-dev.yaml`) syncs only `dev/`, which holds the Secret. The Deployment manifests that pull the image into `maglo-integration` are in neither repository (not verified), and `k8s/` is not referenced by ArgoCD.

# 8. Folder tree with one-line descriptions

Every folder of both repositories (`__pycache__` folders omitted). File counts are non-test `.py` files unless the
line says "tests for". Sources: the survey folder descriptions merged per path, plus a directory listing of each
folder that had no description.

```
makun-ai-feature-dev-2026/  -- main service repo; root holds pyproject.toml, poetry.lock, Dockerfile, docker-compose.yml, alembic.ini, .gitmodules, .env.example, .dockerignore, .gitignore, empty README.md
  .github/  -- GitHub configuration (workflows only)
    workflows/  -- build.yaml (poetry install on push/PR, test steps commented out) and deployment.yaml (build+push image on branch feature--dev--2026)

# 9. Learning order (easy -> hard)

This list groups the 494 non-test source files into 22 "pieces". Each piece is one folder or one group of files that you read together. The order follows three rules. First, a piece comes after every piece it imports from, with two forward references noted inline: the LLM engine (piece 10) reads the tool schema from piece 11, and campaign (piece 17) calls `VoiceService.initiate_call` from piece 18. The Celery piece (8) is placed before the packages whose `tasks.py` it imports, because those packages import the Celery app themselves (see piece 8). Second, within the same dependency level, the piece with the lower survey difficulty comes first. Third, the voice packages come last because they are the largest (22,719 lines, verified), they import from almost every other package, and the surveys rate most of their big files at difficulty 5. Line counts below were measured with `wc -l` (non-test `.py` files only unless stated). Difficulty values are the 1-5 scores from the survey JSON files for the main file of each piece.

1. **Configuration and environment.** `ENV` is one class whose ~120 class attributes are read with `os.getenv` when the module is imported (`src/core/environment.py:1-415`). Nothing else in the service runs without it, and 65 non-test files under `src/`, `alembic/` and `scripts/` import it (grep verified; 68 with tests).
   - Read: `src/core/environment.py` (415 lines; skim the attribute names, then read the URL builders `MONGO_DATABASE_URL` / `POSTGRES_DATABASE_URL` / `CACHE_DATABASE_URL` at `:335-349` and `validate_env_vars` at `:371`), `src/core/app_env.py` (29), `src/config/` (4 files, 133 lines: `settings.py` 49, `log.py` 50, `__init__.py` 18, `swagger.py` 16), `src/database/constants.py` (59, real table names), `src/routes/constants.py` (1).
   - Why here: no project imports; difficulty 2. Note `src/core/environment.py:1` (`from curses.ascii import US`) is unused and breaks import on Windows (verified).
   - After: you can say where every setting, hostname and secret NAME comes from, and which ones have hardcoded defaults (values are redacted in these docs).

2. **Response envelope, errors, routes, middleware and identity.** Every controller returns one of the classes in `src/components/common/responses.py` (18 classes, `:13-425`), and every request passes through the six middlewares listed in `src/middlewares/__init__.py:30-43`. Identity (`request.state.user`) is set by `AuthMiddleware` from one of three headers: `API-KEY` (`src/middlewares/authentication.py:57`), `X-Agent-Secret` (`:69`) or `Authorization: Bearer` (`:89`), with the Console service consulted over gRPC (Google Remote Procedure Call) when Redis has no cached answer.
   - Read: `src/components/common/responses.py` (519), `request_identity.py` (97), `channel.py` (120, `Interface`/`Medium` enums), `src/exceptions/__init__.py` (124), `src/routes/base.py` (64, `register_all_routes` at `:39`), `src/middlewares/` (7 files, 806 lines: `authentication.py` 234, `exception.py` 281, `logging_request_and_response.py` 140, `allowed_host.py` 57, `context.py` 43, `__init__.py` 43), `src/loggers/base.py` (124), `src/grpc_client/` (7 files, 419: `grpc_client.py` 96, `client_services/auth_service_client.py` 210, `api_key_service_client.py` 74) and `src/grpc_interceptor/` (26).
   - Why here: depends only on piece 1; difficulty 2 for responses, 4 for `authentication.py`. The middleware order (ContextMiddleware first, logging last, verified against starlette 0.46.2 `build_middleware_stack`) is needed to read any controller.
   - After: you can read any route handler, know what `get_partner_id(request)` returns and why the WebSocket route gets no middleware except context.

3. **DI container, database managers and app assembly.** `Container` (`src/core/container.py:857`) is a `dependency-injector` (locked 4.49.0) `DeclarativeContainer` whose providers call 91 module-level `_provide_*` factory functions (`:17-849`, count verified with grep). `src/main.py` creates the container (`:24`), registers routers (`:33-35`) and runs startup work in a deprecated `@app.on_event("startup")` handler (`:58-82`): `init_resources`, template seeding, partner-config sync to Redis, constants initialisation.
   - Read: `src/core/container.py` (1374; start at `:857-905`, then trace one chain such as `agent_repository` -> `_provide_agent_repo`), `src/core/db.py` (88, `DatabaseSessionManager`), `src/core/doc_db.py` (147, Motor/MongoDB client), `src/core/redis.py` (30), `src/database/base.py` (23, the `MetaData` abstract model most tables inherit), `src/main.py` (87).
   - Why here: needs pieces 1-2; difficulty 4 for the container. DI (dependency injection) is the pattern every service and task uses, so it must come before any feature package.
   - After: you can resolve any `Depends(Provide[Container.x])` to its constructor and its dependencies, and you know which providers are Singleton, Factory or Resource (`redis_pool` is a Resource at `:903`, so anything that depends on it must be awaited).

4. **Agents: the clean CRUD example.** CRUD (create, read, update, delete) for the `makun_ai_agents` table, laid out as controller -> service -> repository -> ORM (object-relational mapper) model, which is the house pattern. `AgentService.load_agent_config` is a Redis cache-aside loader that chat, voice and campaign all call at runtime (survey, agents slice).
   - Read in order: `src/components/agents/constants.py` (209), `errors.py` (137), `models.py` (262), `schemas.py` (379, `AgentConfig` is the runtime shape), `repositories.py` (995), `static_methods.py` (202, prompt assembly), `services.py` (1209), `controllers.py` (813, 17 routes under `/ai/v1/agents`). Package total 13 files, 4,318 lines.
   - Why here: first feature package; it depends only on pieces 1-3. Difficulty 3 for models/controllers, 4 for repositories, 5 for services (read `load_agent_config` and `build_llm_request` slowly).
   - After: you can add a CRUD feature in the same style, and you know what an `AgentConfig` contains before you meet it again in chat and voice.

5. **Partner config, limits, common helpers and the dead RBAC files.** `PartnerConfig` rows hold per-partner rate limits and are mirrored into Redis (`src/components/partner_config/services.py:375`); `limits.py` is a pure resolver with no I/O. `src/components/common/http_client.py` is the shared `httpx` (locked 0.28.1) wrapper used by tools, integrations and campaign. RBAC (role-based access control) under `src/components/rbac/` is not imported by any non-test module (grep verified).
   - Read: `src/components/partner_config/` (10 files, 2,012 lines: `limits.py` 214, `models.py` 84, `schemas.py` 596, `repositories.py` 326, `services.py` 375, `controllers.py` 224), `src/components/common/http_client.py` (604), `datetime.py` (132), `pagination.py` (16), `context_injection.py` (42), `src/components/rbac/` (4 files, 104 lines, recognise and skip), `src/components/common/user_hierarchy.py` (98, unused per survey).
   - Why here: difficulty 2-3; chat (piece 7) reads these limits, so they must come first.
   - After: you know how a partner's chat and session limits are resolved and where the Redis mirror keys are written.

6. **Sessions.** One Postgres row per (partner_id, session_id) in `makun_ai_chat_sessions`, created with `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (`src/components/sessions/repositories.py`, survey), marked idle by a Celery beat task.
   - Read: `src/components/sessions/` (9 files, 1,170 lines: `models.py` 154, `schemas.py` 73, `repositories.py` 378, `services.py` 306, `controllers.py` 85, `tasks.py` 91).
   - Why here: depends on pieces 3-5; difficulty 3-4. Both chat and voice upsert a session row before doing anything else.
   - After: you know the session lifecycle (active -> idle -> closed) and the counters chat increments.

7. **Chat and media attachments.** `POST /ai/v1/chat/message` (`src/components/chat/controllers.py:48`) returns a `ChatStreamResponse` (SSE, Server-Sent Events, `src/components/common/responses.py:425`) whose generator is `ChatService.stream_chat_message` (`src/components/chat/services.py:586`). Attachments are uploaded through a second router in `chat/media/` and turned into provider content blocks. WhatsApp text arrives through this same endpoint with `interface=whatsapp` (`src/components/common/channel.py:16`); the only WhatsApp-related inbound webhook in the repo is the babbler fallback reply (`src/components/campaign/controllers/webhooks.py:28`), which is campaign code, not chat.
   - Read: `src/components/chat/` (20 files, 4,712 lines including `media/`): `constants.py`, `schemas.py` (199), `models.py` (111), `errors.py`, `rate_limits.py` (209, pure functions), `controllers.py` (246), `repositories.py` (403, MongoDB transcript queries), `services.py` (1137); then `media/` (9 files, 2,059 lines: `constants.py` 75, `helpers.py` 318, `preprocessor.py` 308, `services.py` 627, `content_blocks.py` 448, `controllers.py` 171).
   - Why here: the first end-to-end flow; it needs pieces 2-6. Difficulty 5 for `services.py` (idempotency keys, per-session locks, limit enforcement, event mapping).
   - After: you can follow a chat message from the HTTP layer to the call into `LLMEngine.run_turn`, and read every SSE event type.

8. **Celery: the app, the config and every tasks.py.** One Celery (locked 5.6.3) app with Redis as broker and result backend (`src/makunai_celery/celery_app.py`), explicit `task_routes` (`celery_config.py:18-31`), `task_default_queue = "default_queue"` (`:33`) and an 8-entry `beat_schedule` (`:49`). The route pattern `src.makunai_celery.tasks.*` matches no real task because all 14 tasks live in `src.components.*` (verified). Each component's `tasks.py` builds its own event loop and dependencies by hand.
   - Read: `src/makunai_celery/` (5 files, 233 lines: `celery_utils.py` 49, `celery_app.py` 31, `celery_config.py` 104, `tasks.py` 49), then `src/components/sessions/tasks.py` (91, the simplest task; its package is piece 6). The other `tasks.py` files belong to packages you have not read yet, so read each one when you reach its piece: `context/tasks.py` (93, piece 9), `integrations/tasks.py` (75, piece 12), `orchestration/tasks.py` (42, piece 13), `rag/tasks.py` (287, piece 14), `campaign/tasks.py` (540, piece 17, the `_async_wrapper`/`_run` harness at `:22-79`).
   - Why here: difficulty 2 for config, 4 for the campaign and rag tasks. It comes here because the context package (piece 9) imports the Celery app at module level (`src/components/context/session_memory.py:8` -> `context/tasks.py:5` -> `src/makunai_celery/celery_app.py`) and the tool executor (piece 11) enqueues a task (`executor.py:1478`), so the Celery app must be known before them. The hub `src/makunai_celery/tasks.py` in turn imports every component `tasks.py`; that is the one cycle no reading order can remove.
   - After: you can predict which queue any task lands in and which worker pool consumes it.

9. **Context and history manager.** `ContextOrchestrator.build_context` (`src/components/context/context_orchestrator.py:64`) assembles what the model sees: a sliding window of MongoDB history, a Redis hot tier, session memory facts, summaries and optional RAG chunks. `MongoHistoryManager.append_messages` enqueues `compress_session` when `ENV.COMPRESSION_ENABLED` is set (`src/components/history_manager/manager.py:149-159`).
   - Read: `src/components/context/` (10 files, 1,236 lines: `policies.py` 61, `schemas.py`, `hot_tier.py` 101, `session_memory.py` 170, `context_orchestrator.py` 292, `compression.py` 198, `repository.py` 196, `tasks.py` 93), `src/components/history_manager/` (3 files, 294: `interfaces.py`, `manager.py` 256).
   - Why here: it sits between chat (piece 7) and the LLM core (piece 10), and `session_memory.py:8` imports the Celery app through `context/tasks.py:5`, so it comes after piece 8; difficulty 4.
   - After: you can explain exactly which messages reach the model for a given session and where the two Celery producers (`persist_session_fact.delay` at `session_memory.py:147`, `compress_session.delay` at `manager.py:159`) are called.

10. **LLM engine, core and providers.** LLM means large language model. `LLMCore.generate_response_stream` (`src/components/llm/core.py:162`) streams one model call and buffers tool-call fragments; `LLMEngine.run_turn` (`src/components/llm/engine.py:109`) is the loop around it that executes tools and re-calls the model. Providers share one `StreamDelta` contract (`providers/base.py`). Groq is reached through the `openai` SDK (locked 2.31.0) with a custom base URL; there is no `groq` package in the lock (verified).
    - Read: `src/components/llm/` (18 files, 6,007 lines): `constants.py` (348), `schemas.py` (325), `providers/base.py` (133), `providers/openai_llm.py` (355), then `groq_llm.py` (314), `gemini_llm.py` (321), `anthropic_llm.py` (375) as diffs, `helpers.py` (273), `factory.py` (400), `core.py` (1344), `engine.py` (1058).
    - Why here: needs the history contract from piece 9 and the tool contract from piece 11 (a forward reference: read `src/components/tools/schemas.py` early). Difficulty 5 for `core.py` and `engine.py`.
    - After: you can add a provider, and you can read the tool-call buffering and dedup logic that both chat and voice rely on (`LLMEngine` is imported by `src/components/voice/adapters/llm_adapter.py:13`, `src/components/voice/session/agent.py` and `ws_relay_session.py`, grep verified).

11. **Tools.** A tool is a row in `makun_ai_tools` with a JSON schema; `ToolExecutor.execute_tool` (`src/components/tools/executor.py:280`) turns a model tool call into an HTTP call, an MCP (Model Context Protocol) call, an internal call or a sub-agent delegation, and logs it through Celery (`write_invocation_log.delay` at `:1478`).
    - Read: `src/components/tools/` (23 files, 5,920 lines): `constants.py`, `models.py` (293), `schemas.py` (267), `repositories.py` (600), `services.py` (170), `controllers.py` (286), `credentials/` (7 files, 1,004 lines), `clients/service_clients.py` (116), `clients/mcp_client.py` (350), `auth_resolver.py` (252), `executor.py` (1596, read `execute_tool` then `_execute_http_tool`, `_execute_integration_tool`, `_execute_internal_tool`).
    - Why here: difficulty 5 for `executor.py`; it is called from piece 10 and calls into piece 12; it also enqueues the piece 13 task `write_invocation_log` through the Celery app from piece 8.
    - After: you can trace any tool call from the model's JSON to the outbound request and back.

12. **Integrations.** Third-party connections: encrypted credentials (`CredentialVault`, AES-256-GCM, `src/components/integrations/core/vault.py:40-96`), OAuth2 (Open Authorization 2) with PKCE (Proof Key for Code Exchange) in `services/credentials.py`, provider templates, MCP tool discovery, and `IntegrationHandler.execute` (`handler.py:66`) which the tool executor calls.
    - Read: `src/components/integrations/` (37 files, 9,276 lines): `constants.py` (283), `models.py` (286), `core/` (6 files, 1,023: `vault.py` 466, `credential_access.py` 280, `oauth2_client.py` 88), `repositories/` (`credentials.py` 617, `partner_integrations.py` 701), `services/credentials.py` (560), `adapters/` (5 files, 750: `http_generic.py` 418, `mcp_generic.py` 124), `mcp/dcr_service.py` (400), `handler.py` (316), `services/agent_integrations.py` (1884, read in sections), `controllers/`, `tasks.py` (75).
    - Why here: largest non-voice package after campaign; difficulty 5 for `agent_integrations.py`. It needs pieces 11 and 8 (one beat task).
    - After: you can follow "connect account" from the OAuth init route to a stored, refreshed credential used by a tool call.

13. **Orchestration (sub-agents).** `SubAgentRunner.run` (`src/components/orchestration/sub_agent_runner.py:299`) runs a child agent's `LLMEngine` turn when the parent calls the synthetic `delegate_to_agent` tool; invocations are logged by the `write_invocation_log` task.
    - Read: `src/components/orchestration/` (8 files, 732 lines: `constants.py`, `schemas.py`, `exceptions.py`, `utils.py`, `sub_agent_runner.py` 463, `models.py` 54, `repositories.py` 56, `tasks.py` 42).
    - Why here: small, but it needs pieces 10 and 11; difficulty 4.
    - After: you know how one agent calls another and what the recursion limits are.

14. **RAG (retrieval-augmented generation).** Documents are uploaded, parsed, chunked, embedded and stored in Postgres with `pgvector` (locked 0.3.6); the Celery task `process_document_ingestion` (`src/components/rag/tasks.py:25`) does the work and is enqueued from `rag/services.py:114,342,514`. Retrieval is consumed by the context orchestrator (piece 9) and the tool executor (piece 11).
    - Read: `src/components/rag/` (28 files, 3,572 lines): `constants.py` (124), `models.py` (122), `schemas.py`, `controllers.py` (464, 15 routes), `services.py` (589), `tasks.py` (287), `ingestion/parsers/` (7 files) and `ingestion/chunkers/` (3 files), 483 lines together with `ingestion/__init__.py`, `embeddings/` (3 files, 98), `repositories.py` (669, finish with `search`), `retrieval/` (3 files, 302).
    - Why here: self-contained pipeline; difficulty 3-4. It needs piece 8 for the task harness.
    - After: you can add a parser or change chunking, and you know where vector search enters a chat turn.

15. **Prompt builder.** Prompt templates stored in `makun_ai_agent_prompt_templates`, rendered with `render_prompt` (`src/components/prompt_builder/renderer.py:27`), plus an SSE "generate a prompt" endpoint that calls the LLM factory. `constants.py` is 1,247 lines of seed prompt text and interface guidance.
    - Read: `src/components/prompt_builder/` (14 files, 4,329 lines): `constants.py` (1247, read only `:1-191` and one seed dict), `models.py` (204), `schemas.py`, `renderer.py` (103, also used by agents and voice), `services.py` (392), `repositories.py` (861), `meta_prompts.py` (152), `llm_adapter.py` (108), `utils.py`, `controllers.py` (428), `template_initializer.py` (seeded at startup from `src/main.py:62-63`).
    - Why here: depends on piece 10; difficulty 3-4.
    - After: you can add a template category and know how `{current_date}`-style variables are filled.

16. **Skin analysis.** A vertical feature: images -> OpenCV face detection (`face_detector.py`, bundled ONNX model) -> vision model report (`engines/llm_vision_engine.py`) -> annotated image -> optional image simulation. It is self-contained and reaches the rest of the code only through the tool executor and the digital_assets submodule.
    - Read: `src/components/skin_analysis/` (19 files, 3,774 lines): `constants.py` (308), `schemas.py` (199), `ports.py`, `engines/llm_vision_engine.py` (196), `face_detector.py` (192), `image_annotation_renderer.py` (420), `services.py` (892, start with `_run_report_phase`, then `analyze` at `:143`), `engines/gpt_image_simulation_engine.py` (156), `repositories.py` (329), `models.py` (174), `controllers.py` (433).
    - Why here: difficulty 3-4; isolated, so it can be skipped on a first pass. It uses `opencv-python-headless` (locked 4.14.0.94) and `pillow` (locked 10.4.0).
    - After: you can change a prompt or threshold without touching chat or voice.

17. **Campaign and CDR (call detail records).** The outbound dialer: a campaign is published and started, recipients are materialised from lead lists, `dispatch_coordinator` -> `dispatch_campaign_tick` (`src/components/campaign/tasks.py:301`) runs `DispatchService.dispatch_tick` (`services/dispatch.py:308`) which claims recipients and places calls through the Tata dialer; CDR webhooks (`controllers/webhooks.py:146,170`) update recipient state through `DispositionMappingService`; failed leads can fall back to a WhatsApp message via babbler-service and the babbler reply webhook (`:28`).
    - Read: `src/components/campaign/` (63 files, 11,093 lines): `constants.py` (242, `RecipientStatus`, Redis keys), `models/` (`recipient.py` 83, `campaign.py` 121), `schemas/`, `controllers/` (9 files, 1,652: `campaign.py` 600, `webhooks.py` 224), `services/` (20 files, 5,426: `campaign.py` 1353, `dispatch.py` 1335, `disposition_mapping.py` 491, `did_selection.py` 305, `tata_cdr_mapper.py` 160, `lead_list.py` 343, `fallback_flow.py` 147, `fallback_engine.py` 119, `babbler_client.py` 329, `node_handlers/whatsapp.py`), `repositories/` (9 files, 1,957: `recipient.py` 689, `campaign.py` 343), `tasks.py` (540); `src/components/cdr/` (4 files, 320: `models.py` 98, `repositories.py` 112, `services.py` 110).
    - Why here: second-largest package, difficulty 5 for `dispatch.py` (concurrency is all `UPDATE ... WHERE status = expected RETURNING`). It needs pieces 4, 5, 8 and the voice call-initiation path from piece 18 (a forward reference), so read `VoiceService.initiate_call` in `src/components/voice/services.py` alongside.
    - After: you can follow one lead from CSV upload to a disposition code and explain every Redis key the dispatcher uses.

18. **Voice core: HTTP tier, worker and pipeline.** `VoiceService.initiate_call` (`src/components/voice/services.py`) writes a `makun_ai_call_sessions` row, builds `RoomMetadata` (the API-to-worker contract in `schemas.py`) and creates a LiveKit room. The worker process (`worker.py`, `entrypoint` at `:716`, `prewarm_process` at `:249`, `cli.run_app` at `:755`) is a separate `livekit-agents` (locked 1.8.2) process. `VoicePipelineBuilder.build_session` (`pipeline/builder.py:96`) turns `RoomMetadata` plus `AgentConfig` into an `AgentSession`. The WebSocket route `/ai/v1/voice/stream/{agent_id}` (`controllers.py:403`) is protected only by the HMAC (hash-based message authentication code) ticket from `platform/stream_token.py`.
    - Read: `src/components/voice/constants/` (7 files, 539 lines: `session.py`, `profiles.py`, `providers.py`, `timing.py`, `fillers.py`, `voicemail.py`), `schemas.py` (162), `models.py` (80), `errors.py`, `messages.py`, `repositories.py` (341), `controllers.py` (462), `services.py` (762), `platform/stream_token.py` (66), `pipeline/` (5 files, 981: `config_resolver.py` 205, `registry.py` 125, `builder.py` 522, `timing.py`), `ports/` (3 files, 107), `policies.py` (36), `worker.py` (774).
    - Why here: difficulty 3 for controllers, 4 for services and builder, 5 for `worker.py` (forkserver preloads). It needs pieces 2-6 and 10.
    - After: you know the two voice entry paths (LiveKit room and WebSocket relay) and what the worker receives.

19. **Voice adapters: STT, TTS, VAD, realtime, LiveKit and the LLM bridge.** STT is speech-to-text, TTS is text-to-speech, VAD is voice activity detection. Each adapter's `build_plugin()` returns a LiveKit plugin with an API key from `ENV`. `LLMCoreAdapter` (`adapters/llm_adapter.py:49`) implements LiveKit's `llm.LLM` interface on top of `LLMEngine`; `LLMTurnCore` (`llm_core_bridge.py:48`) is a framework-independent twin used in shadow mode.
    - Read: `src/components/voice/adapters/` (37 files, 4,090 lines): `base_adapter.py`, `stt/` (6 files, 265: `deepgram_stt.py` 133), `tts/` (11 files, 1,175: `cartesia_tts.py` 129), `vad/` (5 files, 95: `silero_vad.py` 68), `realtime/` (5 files, 572: `gemini_realtime.py` 278), `livekit/` (`base.py` 36, `livekit_transport.py` 193, `livekit_runtime.py` 103), `llm_adapter.py` (965), `llm_core_bridge.py` (621).
    - Why here: the session piece imports every adapter, so the adapters come first; difficulty 4-5 for the two LLM files, 2-3 for the provider adapters, which you can read on their own if you only need to swap a vendor.
    - After: you can add a TTS or STT vendor and explain how a voice turn reuses the chat LLM stack.

20. **Voice session: the in-call state machine.** `VoiceSessionHandler` (`src/components/voice/session/handler.py:52`) composes three mixins: `SessionLifecycleMixin` (`lifecycle.py:37`, `setup` at `:39`, `run` at `:229`, `_finalize` at `:1490`), `SessionEventRouterMixin` (`event_router.py:24`, `register_event_hooks` at `:25`) and `SessionBillingMixin` (`billing.py`; class header at `handler.py:52-56`); the transcript capture helpers are imported functions and classes (`handler.py:28-47`), not mixed in. `MakunVoiceAgent` (`agent.py:223`) is the `livekit.agents.Agent` subclass where a caller utterance becomes an LLM turn (`on_user_turn_completed` at `:874`). `WsRelaySession` (`ws_relay_session.py:189`, 3,077 lines, the largest file in the repo) is the non-LiveKit loop for the Tata PSTN (public switched telephone network) relay.
    - Read: `src/components/voice/session/` (13 files, 8,572 lines): `handler.py` (1188), `lifecycle.py` (1572), `event_router.py` (627), `agent.py` (1081), `history_sync.py` (243), `transcript_export.py`, `response_cache.py`, `realtime_error_taxonomy.py` (138), `realtime_error_alerts.py`, `realtime_metrics.py`, `billing.py`, then `ws_relay_session.py` (3077: module docstring, `run` at `:258`, `_run_cascaded` at `:1480`, `_run_speech_to_speech` at `:1797`).
    - Why here: difficulty 5 for lifecycle, agent, event_router and ws_relay_session. It needs pieces 18 and 19 (read `adapters/base_adapter.py` first).
    - After: you can follow a call from room join to finalisation, including reconnects and realtime-model error recovery.

21. **Voice transcript: turn accumulation and structured capture (hardest).** Two generations coexist. The new `TranscriptEngine.ingest` (`transcript/engine.py:118`) composes a `TurnBuffer` (`core/buffer.py:32`, `update` at `:146`), a commit guard, a continuation strategy and a language profile. The legacy `PendingTurnBuffer` / `continuations.py` path is still what `handler.py` drives, and the survey reports the engine verdict is discarded (voice-transcript slice, learning-order step 9). `core/spoken_format.py` (707 lines) converts spoken digits, emails and dates to text without an LLM.
    - Read: `src/components/voice/transcript/` (37 files, 5,499 lines): `core/` (14 files, 2,042: `events.py` 60, `candidate.py` 70, `actions.py`, `normalizer.py` 140, `buffer.py` 290, `spoken_format.py` 707, `capture_fields/` 7 files 696 incl. `date.py` 399), `guards/` (4 files, 417: `profile_guard.py` 235), `languages/` (6 files, 1,482: `base.py` 269, `hinglish.py` 291, `registry.py` 146), `strategies/` (6 files, 416: `standard.py` 95), `engine.py` (371), legacy `continuations.py` (185), `pending_turn.py` (104), `__init__.py` (106, which duplicate names are exported).
    - Why last among code: difficulty 5 for `spoken_format.py` and the handler integration; you must already know piece 20 to see which path is live, and the heuristics are language-specific (Hinglish default).
    - After: you can tune commit thresholds or add a language profile without breaking live calls.

22. **Infrastructure: images, compose, CI, Kubernetes, migrations, the oc-config repo.** `Dockerfile` (22 lines, `python:3.11-slim-bookworm`, `EXPOSE 7000 7001 50051 9090` at `:6`), `docker-compose.yml` (199 lines, 9 services at `:4-141`), CI (continuous integration) in `.github/workflows/build.yaml` (63) and `deployment.yaml` (55, triggers on branch `feature--dev--2026` at `:6`, pushes tag `makun-ai:integrationlatest` at `:49`), `k8s/campaign-dispatch-worker.yaml` (130, two Deployments and two KEDA, Kubernetes Event-driven Autoscaling, ScaledObjects), `alembic/env.py` (124, imports the missing `digital_assets` submodule at `:17`) and `alembic/versions/` (34 files, 3,006 lines, linear chain v1 -> v34). The second repo `makun-ai-oc-config-master` holds an ArgoCD `Application` (`argocd/apps/makun-ai-dev.yaml`, 31 lines: `path: dev` at `:15`, namespace `maglo-integration` at `:18`, `prune`/`selfHeal` at `:21-22`) and a plaintext Kubernetes `Secret` with 56 keys (`dev/makun-ai-env-secret.yaml`, 63 lines; read key names only).
    - Read: the files above plus `pyproject.toml` (70), `poetry.lock` (locked versions), `.env.example`, `.gitmodules`, and `oc-config/.github/workflows/pr-validate.yaml`.
    - Why last: difficulty 2-3, but every file names a process, queue or table you only recognise after pieces 3, 8, 17 and 18. Note the mismatch: the k8s manifest references `secretRef makunai-service-env` (`:49,107`) and image `makunai-service:latest`, neither of which exists in the oc-config Secret (verified).
    - After: you can say which process runs which code, which queue each worker consumes, and how a push on `feature--dev--2026` becomes a running pod.

# 10. The 20% of files that do 80% of the work

The 58 files below are selected from `importance >= 4` in the survey index, each slice's `top_files` list, and the largest-file facts. Line counts are `wc -l` on 2026-10-10. The "flows" column uses these names: **startup** (process boot and request plumbing), **chat** (`POST /ai/v1/chat/message` and attachments), **voice-livekit** (LiveKit room calls run by `worker.py`), **voice-relay** (the `/ai/v1/voice/stream/{agent_id}` WebSocket run by `WsRelaySession`), **campaign** (outbound dialer, CDR webhooks, fallback), **rag** (document ingestion and retrieval), **integrations** (OAuth, credentials, MCP), **jobs** (Celery tasks and beat).

Every `Provide[Container.x]` resolves here; Celery tasks reuse it as `celery.container`. | startup, chat, voice-livekit, voice-relay, campaign, rag, integrations, jobs |
